# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

My eyes at this present writing see hourly, with admiration of the infinite Glory, such a great splendor in these created things. Trust me, it will ravish the beholder to see such a despised infant as our *Mercury* <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the "First Matter" or the soul of metals.</note> is, grow into so strong a *Hero* <note>original: "Heroe"</note> that the world cannot purchase it.

-> But not the common kind, called *Quick-silver* by name. <-

Yet the difficulty is not over once it is known that the whole Secret consists in *Mercury*. For what is more frequent among the *Sophisters* <note>term: Sophisters; here referring to deceptive or ignorant practitioners of alchemy who use false logic.</note> than to cry, "*Our Mercury, etc.*" and yet in the Work of Nature they are as blind as moles? The cause is that Nature has produced a mineral juice in the bowels of the Earth which answers to most of the philosophical descriptions of their Water. For example, it is mineral, quick, current, without wetting <note>original: "humectation"</note>, heavy, and the like. When the common *Alchemists* read this, they apply it to this naughty <note>In this context, "naughty" means worthless or inferior.</note> *Mercury*, which for in—

<page-num>16</page-num>

ward *Qualities* has nothing in it like ours.

Some there are who, trusting to the opinions of most of the wise men who have written concerning this Art, do reject *common mercury* <note>original: "Mercury vulgar"</note> in word, even while they dote upon it as much as others in practice. They perform mock-purifications, handling *mercury* in various ways through sublimation, precipitation, and manual calcination <note>The process of reducing a metal to a powder by heat or chemical action</note>—even reducing it to a black substance like soot or lamp-black. They attempt distillation from various *dregs* <note>original: "Faces" (faeces)</note> after grinding it with vinegar, or by calcination with *strong waters* <note>original: "Waters-fort" (aqua fortis/nitric acid)</note>, or through innumerable washings, changing *mercury* into various forms and then "quickening" it <note>Restoring it to its liquid, metallic state</note> again. By all these operations, they imagine themselves secure in the secret of our *Mercury*, when in fact all such methods are merely *deceptions* <note>original: "Sophisms"</note>; for *mercury* so abused remains one and the same common *mercury*.

So it is that more have stumbled upon this rock than upon any other, and they will continue to stumble until they know how to distinguish our *Mercury* from the common kind, and our preparations from those of the common *deceivers* <note>original: "Sophisters"</note>.

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

...matters, which have no likeness to one another.
*But Mercury, without which nothing exists.* <note>original: "Mercury, without which nothing being is."</note>

For our <term>Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, "Mercury" refers not just to the liquid metal, but to the "spirit" or fluid principle of matter.</note> is essential and radical to our Body, and partakes of its nature entirely. Therefore, it is said to be that Mercury without which nothing exists; for all things are distinguished by Philosophers by three Principles (although some simple substances do not have all three, except by analogy). Among these, the most essential is Mercury, for the humidity of all concrete things is called their Mercury. This is most integral to all things, because all things owe their beginning to Water.

So then, just as the proper, specific Mercury of all things is so essential to them that nothing exists without it, so our Mercury is so <term>consubstantial</term> <note>of the same substance or essence</note> with our Body that it is of the same kind as that Mercury from which it was formed by <term>coagulation</term> <note>the process of a liquid becoming solid</note>. Common <term>vulgar Mercury</term> <note>quicksilver found in shops</note> is not like this. Therefore, the Body is <term>incrudated</term> <note>returned to a raw or liquid state</note> by this Mercury...

They are most injurious, therefore, to the well-deserving philosophers. Because these searchers cannot understand their writings, and through a misunderstanding of the possibilities of Nature, they commit foul mistakes in their operations. Consequently, they reap a ridiculous harvest. They then blame the falsehood of the authors, or at least accuse them of writing with difficulty. They do not consider that philosophers owe them nothing. Whatever they write for the information of the studious is not out of debt, nor yet out of covetousness <note>original: "Covetoufnefs"; meaning greed or a desire for wealth</note>—for they already possess the greatest treasure in the world. ### The Preface of Sir George Ripley

<margin>Reward for our faithfulness.</margin>
...unto us for our faithfulness in this matter. This reward we shall receive from the Sons of this Science <note>original: "Sons of this Science"; a term for initiated alchemists or dedicated students of the craft</note>, whatever we may receive from others. Therefore, our books are intended for the former; we do not write a single word for the latter.

<margin>Unlearned men are not to meddle with this Science.</margin>
But those who are not scholars <note>original: "Clerks"; meaning educated men or members of the clergy</note> and yet meddle with philosophy are blameworthy. 

Moreover, we do not write our books for the information of the illiterate, as though any common mechanical distiller, alchemist, or deceiver <note>original: "Sophister"</note> should easily carry away the Golden Fleece <note>A mythological reference to the prize sought by Jason and the Argonauts, here symbolizing the Great Work of alchemy</note>. Nor do we write so that any covetous man, who makes profit his ultimate goal, should easily gather the Apples of the Hesperides <note>In Greek mythology, golden apples that granted immortality</note>. Nor yet do we write so that anyone, even if they are learned, should be made a philosopher straight away by reading over the text once or twice in a superficial manner (just as dogs lap the water of the Nile <note>original: "Nilus"; a reference to a proverb about doing something quickly or distractedly</note>). No, truly, the majesty of this science forbids such great impiety; it is the gift of God, and not of men. Our books are for those who have been, or intend to be, familiar with the search for Nature; we hint at the...

## The Preface of Sir George Ripley

*Yet wisely understand what it is,*
*and where you shall seek it.*

Therefore, let me advise every studious searcher of this hidden <term>science</term> <note>original: "Science"; here referring to Alchemy</note> to consider warily within himself what he seeks and wishes to find. Not only that, but he must consider in what substance he expects to find it. For trust me, it is not in this science as some imagine—that our <term>secret</term> <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> may be made out of just anything, nor even out of any base or common thing. But in the knowing of the true <term>principle</term> <note>the fundamental starting material</note> consists the first true step to perfection, according to the poet: *He who has begun well has already finished half the work.* <note>original Latin: "Dimidium facti qui bene coepit habet"</note>

*Otherwise, I counsel you, do not take this work in hand.*

But he who does not know this, our <term>ocean</term> in which our <term>water</term> has its <term>ebb</term> and <term>flow</term> <note>original: "flux and reflux"</note>, and our <term>fountain</term> out of which he may draw this water for his use, let him avoid this as a most dangerous science; for he can only expect loss in it, but no profit.

<summary>Sir George Ripley warns aspiring alchemists that success depends entirely on identifying the correct starting material, rather than attempting to create the "Arcanum" from random or base substances.</summary>

### For *Philosophers* flatter *Fools* with fair Speech.

Nor should anyone expect comfortable direction in our books if they do not know the true Matter, nor the true Keys by which our Matter is brought forth from darkness into the light. For truly, though we write for the enlightening of a son of Art <note>original: "son of Art"; a term for a true initiate or student of alchemy</note>, we also write for the fatal blinding of all such "owls" and "bats" who cannot behold the light of the Sun, nor endure the splendor of our Moon <note>The Sun and Moon here represent the primary masculine and feminine principles of alchemy, often associated with Gold and Silver or Sulfur and Mercury</note>. To such people, we propose rare tricks suited to their sordid fancies: to the covetous, an easy way without expense in a very short time; to the lazy scholars, a mere game without tedious toil; and to the unstable, rash, and hasty, a multiplicity of distillations.

### But listen to me, for truly I will teach you.

But to you—supposing your qualifications to be honesty, secrecy, studiousness, and indefatigableness—we will show the Truth; yet we will do so in such a way that it may be hidden...

## The Preface of Sir George Ripley
### Hidden from the common people, yet plain enough to an industrious, attentive reader:
### *Which is this most profitable Mercury?*

Philosophers have hidden much under the <term>homonym</term> <note>original: "Homonymium"; a word that sounds the same but has different meanings</note> of "Mercury," so that it is not difficult for those who read their books to misunderstand them. Indeed, as many as God wills to be excluded from this art shall certainly be mistaken.

For many things are named by them with the name of "Mercury" which are altogether useless in this <term>mastery</term> <note>The "Mastery" or "Magistery" refers to the successful completion of the Great Work in alchemy</note>; and they have described many processes which they themselves never actually performed. For my part, I shall not follow their metaphorical footsteps, but shall candidly follow the path of the profound Ripley, whose text I attach to my discourse as I go. I do this because it is an elaborate piece written with excellent method. I do not so much comment on him—for I am writing from my own experimental knowledge—but rather I intend this treatise to be a light to that excellent light in alchemy. These labors of mine are complete in themselves; I provide them only to help you to my utmost...

<summary>The author warns that alchemical writers often use the term "Mercury" deceptively to mislead the unworthy, promising instead to provide a clear, experimental guide based on the works of Sir George Ripley.</summary>
<keywords>Sir George Ripley, Mercury, Alchemy, Mastery, Chemical Philosophy, Hermeticism</keywords>

to the utmost, I have confined my discourse to his method, which I might (as other philosophers have done) have scattered here and there confusedly.

->Being to you nothing deceptive.<-

As then I have chosen *Ripley's Method* <note>George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was a famous English alchemist; his "Compound of Alchemy" organized the process into twelve "gates" or stages.</note> to follow, so will I imitate his ingenuity, and do solemnly profess not to be deceptive to you in anything. However, I shall not so unfold the mysteries that bare reading alone shall suffice to show the unveiled *Diana* <note>original: "Diana"; a mythological reference used here to represent the pure, hidden secret of the Alchemical Work.</note>.

Know therefore assuredly, that when the philosophers say that their *Matter* is everywhere, etc., they speak this only for the blinding of all those who—taking the philosophers' meaning according to the bare sound of their words—do reap trifles instead of treasures. I shall therefore let you understand that this subject of the philosophers is considered either in reference to its matter or its formal virtue <term>virtue: the inherent power or essential property of a substance</term>. In reference to the former, it is a concrete of water, as all other compounds are; in respect of the latter, it participates

### The Preface of Sir George Ripley

It participates in a celestial virtue, and that in a high degree in both respects. It is said to be in every place: for the original matter, which is water, passes equally through the whole family of concretes <note>original: "concretes" — solid or physical substances formed by the union of elements</note>. And as for the celestial influence, it is so universal that nothing is hidden from its heat; so that indeed, in this sense, it is said to be everywhere.

Moreover, the <term>Stone</term> being the <term>system</term> <note>original: "system" — here meaning a microcosm or a complete representation</note> of the great world, does in some way or other represent everything which is or can be perceived by man. I mean this in reference to some operation, color, or quality; and therefore the wise have described it by almost all things imaginable, for it has a resemblance to everything in one circumstance or another.

->*It is nearer in some things than in others.*<-

Yet to speak properly for your information, and not to conceal the secret, we profess that there is but one kind in which our <term>Stone</term> is found, and in number it is two. Do not understand me as the <term>philosopher</term> finds things in his first laborious preparation,

preparation; for one of the two subjects—which, being of one kind, enter the supernatural work of the generation of our fiery *<term>Stone</term>* <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>—I say our crude <term>Sperm</term> <note>The "seed" or starting material of the metal</note> flows from a *<term>Trinity</term>* of <term>Substances</term> in one <term>Essence</term>. Of these, two are extracted out of the <term>Earth of their Nativity</term> <note>The raw ore or natural state of the mineral</note> by the third, and then become a pure, milky, *<term>Virgin</term>*-like <term>Nature</term>, drawn from the *<term>Menstruum</term>* <note>A solvent used in alchemy</note> of our sordid *<term>Whore</term>* <note>A term for the crude, unrefined matter or "Materia Prima"</note>.

->Take heed, therefore, to what I write to you.<-

And now I call God to witness that I will show you a great *<term>Mystery</term>*: our *<term>Stone</term>* is in one part of a perfect <term>nature</term>, which we would exalt into a more than most perfect state. For this end, we stand in need of our true *<term>Fountain</term>*, which I have described elsewhere and shall not repeat now. This *<term>Fountain</term>* has three *<term>Springs</term>*, and these are three *<term>Witnesses</term>* which testify to the *<term>Artist</term>* <note>The alchemist</note> of the truth of his proceedings. These are the *<term>Spirit</term>*, the *<term>Water</term>*, and the *<term>Blood</term>*, and these three agree in one <note>A reference to 1 John 5:8, applied here to chemical components</note>. ## The Preface of Sir George Ripley:

The outward shell reaches only so far, but the wise man can behold his hidden, secret center. The blood belongs to our *Green Lion* <note>original: "Green Lyon"; a common alchemical term for raw, unrefined mineral matter or antimony, often associated with a powerful solvent</note>, which is indeed the greenest—or rawest—of the three. This is because it possesses no kind of metallic sulfur <note>original: "Metalline Sulphur"; in alchemy, sulfur represents the active, combustible, and coloring principle of metals</note>, not even a single grain. Therefore, it is *totally volatile* <note>tending to evaporate or change state easily</note>, and it is rawer than common water. 

Yet, it is called "the blood" for a most secret reason: because it is the seat of the life, which is the spirit, just as blood in a human being is the seat of his life. Indeed, the spirit is made manifest by this soul of our *Green Lion* and is united to it. So, even though it is very "green" or unripe, that which inhabits it is both pure and ripe; it can and will digest the lion along with the water, making both become "life with life." 

Now, the spirit is nothing else but a chaos <note>original: "Chaos"; here referring to the "materia prima" or the formless state of matter containing all possibilities</note>, the wonder of the wonders of God. Almost every person possesses it and yet does not know it, because as it appears to the world, it is contained in a vile, despised form. Yet it is so useful that in human affairs, no one can do without it. To the philosopher, it appears united to the blood—that is, the blood of our *Green Lion*—which truly is not a "lion" until the spirit...

<summary>This page explains the alchemical symbolism of the "Green Lion" and its "blood," identifying them as the raw but essential carriers of a divine, universal spirit or "chaos."</summary>

And these three agree in one; they are not absolutely one—mark that. Our fire is not made of the matter, and yet it is united with the matter as if it were of one form with it. There is an agreement in one, though not a radical union <note>original: "radical union"; meaning a fundamental or root-level merging of substances</note>; for the spirit (which is the fire) is separable from the water and the blood. Then is our Lion actually Green, but it ceases then to be our Lion, and is instead the true matter to multiply emeralds more glorious than natural ones.

## The Preface of Sir George Ripley.

Then it is most perfect, which Nature alone could never perform; nor is there anything that can thus exalt <term>Tinctures</term> <note>The concentrated essence or medicinal power of a substance</note>, but our dissolving <term>Water</term>, which I told you flows from three <term>Springs</term>. 

The first is a common <term>Well</term> from which everyone draws, and of which <term>Water</term> many people use; this <term>Well</term> has in it a <term>Saturnine drossiness</term> <note>Impurities associated with lead or the raw, unrefined state of matter</note>, which makes the <term>Waters</term> useless. These frigid superfluities are purged by two other <term>Springs</term>, through which the <term>Water</term> of this <term>Well</term> is artificially caused to run. 

These <term>Springs</term> make but one <term>Well</term>, whose <term>Waters</term> appear dry because the humidity is sealed within. The <term>Well</term> itself is surrounded with an <term>Arsenical Wall</term>, and the slimy bottom abounds with the first <term>Ens</term> <note>The "essence" or fundamental being</note> of <term>Mineral Salt</term> and *Sulphur*. These sharpen the <term>Water</term> of the first <term>Well</term>, whose primary quality is <term>Coldness</term>. Being thus sharpened, it becomes so powerful a *Menstruum* <note>original: "Menstruum"; a solvent used to extract or dissolve substances</note>, and so pleasant to the <term>Metals</term>, that for its peculiar <term>Virtue</term> <note>Inherent power or efficacy</note> it is chosen to be the <term>Bath of the Sun and Moon</term> <note>The chemical environment where gold (Sun) and silver (Moon) are dissolved and transformed</note>.

For I will truly now excite you to understand well the three *Mercuries*.

But because one book is never sufficient in this Mastery <note>original: "Maſtery"; refers to the Great Work or the art of alchemy</note> to discover all that is to be known, and other authors write variously of *Mercury*: attend further to what I have to say to you concerning this point.

We have in our work properly three *Mercuries*. One of these is to be prepared by the Philosopher, of which I have already spoken. When this is joined with the perfect Body <note>The "perfect Body" usually refers to gold or silver</note> and set to digest, the glass vessel is sealed. Then, in this first composition, the matter is called *Rebis* <note>original: "Rebis"; from the Latin *res bina*, meaning a "double thing" or "two-fold matter"</note>, that is, "two things" in number; for you may yet separate each from the other in its entire nature.

These two being joined, they operate within the vessel until the compound becomes a black powder, which is then called the "Ashes of the Platter."

This powder dissolves into a black broth, which is called *Elixir*, or water extracted by "elixation" <note>original: "Elixation"; a process of boiling or digesting to extract medicinal or chemical virtues</note>, which is repeated liquefaction.

This Elixir is divided into a more subtle part, which is called *Azoth* <note>original: "Azoth"; a term for the universal solvent or the essential mercury of the philosophers</note>, and the grosser part is called *Leton* <note>original: "Leton"; likely referring to laton, the base metallic body or "black" stage of the matter</note>, which is washed and whitened by the *Azoth*.

In the *Rebis* <note>original: "Rebis"; from the Latin "res bina," meaning a double thing; the hermaphroditic state of the matter where male and female are combined but not yet perfected</note> the matters are confused; in the Elixir they are divided; and in *Azoth* they are conjoined with an inseparable union.

### The Keys of this Science.

These *Menstruums* <note>original: "Menstruums"; in alchemy, these are the solvents or "waters" used to dissolve and transform metals</note> or *Mercuries* are the very keys of this science: the first is the Philosopher's Key, and the other two are Nature's Keys.

->*Raymond Lully calls them his Menstruums.*<-

They are called *Menstruums* by the wise men for three reasons: first, for their secrecy; just as those monthly cycles <note>original: "Lunary Tributes"</note> of women are hidden from common view, so these *Mercuries* are hidden from common seekers. Secondly, for their prognostic signs; just as those cycles in women signify the maturity to conceive, so these are called *Menstruums* because they are fit for pro- <margin>production</margin>

->*Without them, truly, no truth is achieved.*<-

He, then, who does not know the secret of our *menstruums* <note>original: "Menstruals"; in alchemy, a menstruum is a solvent used to dissolve solids, particularly metals</note>, let him refrain from practicing the Work. For truly, he can expect nothing but a deceptive delusion instead of the true work of Nature. He is like a man who would try to enter an inaccessible castle without a key, or shoot a bow without a string.

->*But two of them are superficial.*<-

Now, so that you may know our secrets exactly, we shall faithfully discover our experience to you, as sincerely as a brother may declare to a brother. I shall reveal what I have never yet found revealed in any author.

There are in our *mercury* three *mercurial substances*, which may well be called *menstruums*. The first is the more coarse part (which, although it is a water, is the most tangible and visible part, and so may be termed the "body" of the water). The last is a *fiery form*, which is the...

The Blood of *Cadmus* <note>original: "Cadmus"; in alchemy, this often refers to the metallic or mineral matter that must be "slain" or transformed</note>; this is a real invisible form, which is essentially and formally *Volatile Gold* <note>original: "Sol Volatile"</note>. Know, I say, what it means to be superficial and what it means to be essential. 

You know that Essence is invisible and more of a "form" than a "material"; it is that which activates the matter and ripens it. However, that which is superficial is visible and can be seen; it is more material and passive. Now, those first two which are superficial are the Water and the Blood; the essential *Solvent* is the Spirit. All of these are contained in one, yet they are distinguished by number, though not by their fundamental kind.

### The third Essential to Sun and Moon

So then, two are material passive substances which are united in our sophical <note>original: "sophical" refers to "philosophical," meaning the specialized substances used by alchemists rather than common chemicals.</note> *Mercury*; the third is an active essence which is hidden in our *Mercury*. This essence is essential to the Sun <note>original: "Sun" refers to Gold.</note> and Moon <note>original: "Moon" refers to Silver.</note> because it is a Fire, which is volatile Gold <note>original: "Sol volatile."</note>. As the artist governs this *Mercury*, it will digest the passive principles into either Gold or Silver <note>original: "Sol or Luna"</note> at the philosopher's pleasure.

### Their Properties I will declare right soon.

I shall shortly, in the proper place, describe to you all the properties of these three *Menstrues* <term>Menstrues: Alchemical solvents or "waters" used to dissolve solid materials.</term> when I come to that topic. In the meantime, take notice that by this *Mercury*—in which there are three *Mercuries* or *Menstrues*—the perfect bodies will be calcined <note>original: "calcined" means reduced to a powder through heat.</note> and then dissolved into *Mercury*. At that stage, it is not properly called a *Menstrues* anymore, for it is the fruit itself, called *Azoth* <note>original: "Azoth" is a term for the universal remedy or the essential agent of transformation.</note> or *Virgin's Milk*, which is a state of digestion beyond the initial solvents.

In the first days of the Stone, there appear four elements, of which three are in the sublimed *Mercury* <note>original: "Mercury sublimed"; refers to mercury that has been purified through heating and vaporization</note>, and one is in *Sol* <note>original: "Sol"; Latin for "Sun," representing Gold</note>. This shows that the *Mercury* which Nature has created in the glass <note>the alchemical flask or vessel</note> without our direct intervention is far superior to that *Mercury* which we prepared through laborious toil.

## **The Preface of Sir George Ripley.**

*This is the Stone of which we speak,*
*To whoever understands our writings correctly.*

And truly, he who has well studied our books shall understand that this general <term>Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, Mercury often refers to the "First Matter" or a universal solvent rather than just the liquid metal.</note> which we call <term>Azoth</term> <note>Azoth was considered the "universal remedy" or whole substance of the work, often symbolized by the A and Z (beginning and end).</note> is indeed our **Stone**, which lacks only digestion <note>original: "wanteth only digestion"; meaning it only needs the application of steady heat to mature.</note>, for it is inseparably united. 

This union is not a **Dyptative Conjunction**, which is merely a mixture of the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> with our **Mercury**; nor is it **Triptative**, which is a mixture and union of the **Body**, **Soul**, and **Spirit** occurring before **Putrefaction** <note>The stage of the alchemical process where the matter turns black and "dies" before being reborn.</note>. Instead, it is **Tetraptive**, which is the **Anatization** <note>The equalization or balancing of the four elements/qualities (Hot, Cold, Moist, Dry).</note> of qualities. This is the first degree of the white **Stone**, which will then grow higher and higher until the **Moon** <note>Silver, or the "White" stage of the work.</note> comes up to the full.

*It is a Soul and Substance bright.*

This **Stone**, or its power of multiplication <note>original: "Virtue multiplicative"; the ability of the Stone to transform large quantities of base metal into gold.</note>, is not found in the material itself, but in the form, which causes the matter to receive and afterward impress **Tinctures** <note>The color-changing and transforming properties of the Stone.</note>. For who could believe that **Sol** <note>The Sun or Gold</note>, in which the vir— <note>The text cuts off; likely "virtue" or "virility."</note>

<summary>Sir George Ripley explains that the Alchemical Stone is a specific union of qualities (Tetraptive) rather than a simple mixture, leading to the "White" stage of the Great Work. He emphasizes that the Stone's power to transform metals comes from its "form" rather than its raw material.</summary>

<keywords>Sir George Ripley, Azoth, Mercury, Conjunction, Putrefaction, Anatization, Tinctures, Alchemy, The Stone</keywords>

### Of *Gold* and *Silver* a subtle Influence.

Were it not that this Tincture, which in the *Mercury* is *Gold* <note>original: "Sol"</note> and *Silver* <note>original: "Luna"</note>, were as a Soul—that is, a spiritual thing—it were impossible. It is therefore the very <term>gift of fertility</term> <note>original: "Dos fœcunditatis"</note> which is in Minerals (which appears in their Silver-like and Gold-like Tinctures). This increasing is in some things <term>according to quantity</term> <note>original: "juxta quantitatem"</note>; but in this, it is in quality.

## The Preface of Sir George Ripley

*Whereby the Earth receives resplendence.*

So then, the matter of <term>minerals</term> <note>original: "Minerals"</note> is a dead, passive thing, in which there is included a <term>Light</term> which is clothed in a "vital ethereal breeze" <note>original: "vitali Aura ætheria"</note>, as I may speak. This form of <term>Light</term> is what actuates and specifies—or determines—the matter. 

This splendor or <term>Light</term> is in all <term>metals</term>, such as <term>gold</term> <note>original: "Sol," the sun</note> or <term>silver</term> <note>original: "Luna," the moon</note>. These are more eminently conspicuous in those two perfect bodies, **Gold** and **Silver**, but are more clouded and eclipsed in other <term>mineral</term> bodies by an earthly, drossy <note>original: "faculent," meaning foul or thick with sediment</note> interference between the brightness <note>original: "fulgor"</note> and the <term>superfluities</term> <note>excess or waste material</note>. This interference is the <term>imperfection</term> of such bodies; it is accompanied by a rawness and inconstancy in the **Fire**, where the **impure** carries away the **pure**.

<summary>Sir George Ripley explains that all minerals contain an internal "Light" or vital spirit, which is most visible in gold and silver but obscured by impurities in lesser metals.</summary>
<keywords>Sir George Ripley, alchemy, minerals, Light, Sol, Luna, gold, silver, purification, vital aura</keywords>

# An Exposition upon

For what are *Sol* and *Luna*, says *Avicenna*:
But *Earth* which is pure *White* and *Red*?
<note>original: "Sol and Luna, saith Avicen." Avicenna was a Persian polymath whose writings on alchemy and medicine were foundational in the West.</note>

So then *Sol* and *Luna* <note>Gold and Silver</note> are more formal than material; for the matter is a gross, earthly substance, but the form of Light purifying the substance is a most subtle, spiritual thing. This light ennobles the coarseness of the matter by a fire-abiding <term>Tincture</term> <note>A spiritual or medicinal essence capable of transforming and coloring matter.</note>.

Take from it the said Clearness, and then
That *Earth* will stand but in little stead.

But if this <term>Tincture</term> could be separated from the weight of the matter, the remainder would be an unprofitable earthiness. Our work, therefore, is to advance this Light by exalting it within the matter. While this light in its simple state is only a single unit, it may be brought to a thousand-fold power. It can be gradually so exalted that the matter would seem to be quite swallowed up by the form. And yet, in this exaltation, it is not the mass or the weight that constitutes the Solar or Lunary Virtue <note>The essential power or quality of Gold or Silver</note>, but—

<summary>This page explains that the essence of gold and silver lies in their spiritual "form" or light rather than their physical mass, arguing that the alchemist's goal is to exalt this inner light to a thousand-fold power.</summary>
<keywords>Sol, Luna, Avicenna, Tincture, Matter and Form, Exaltation, Alchemy, Solar Virtue, Lunary Virtue</keywords>

But it is a Light whose multiplication is not in the increase of *weight* <note>original: pondus</note>, but in the circulation of natures, until the Heavenly illuminates the Earthly with an immediate beam, all interposition being removed out of the way.

->**The whole compound is called our Lead.**<-

To attain this admirable multiplication, Philosophers have found a most subtle yet very natural composition, which has been sought after by many. The wise ancients—both to describe the fountain of these mysteries and to hide the secret from the unworthy—have mystically called this their Lead.

->**The quality of clearness comes from the Sun and Moon.**<-

This Lead—so called because of the apparent baseness of its origin—is nonetheless of an admirable power. For it contains the bath for *Sol* and *Luna* <note>original: Sol and Luna; referring to Gold and Silver</note>; that is, the *Sun* and *Moon* enter into it, and

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the initial substance used in the alchemical process, specifically addressing its various names and the common misconceptions regarding its identity.</meta>

<page-num>48</page-num>
<header>An Exposition upon</header>

The philosophers call it *Stinking Water* <note>original: "Aqua fætida"</note>, and some call it the *Filth of Death* <note>original: "Mortis Immundities"</note>. Yet, in truth, it is not unclean in its own nature. Instead, it is made pure—as pure as the skill of the artist can make it with the help of Nature—by joining like-kind with like-kind <note>original: "joyning Consanguinity with Consanguinity"</note>.

->Which is usually named by *Philosophers* their *Green Lion*<-

The philosophers have highly praised and deeply hidden its concealed body, or rather its *Chaos*. They usually call it their *Green Lion*. Many people mistake this and apply the name to *Venus* <note>In alchemy, Venus represents the metal Copper</note>, and some apply it to *Vitriol* <note>Sulfate minerals</note>. These are essentially the same thing, as *Vitriol* is simply *Copper* corroded by an undeveloped <note>original: "embryonated"</note> *Salt*. But "Fools," says George Ripley <note>A famous 15th-century English alchemist</note> in his book *The Erroneous Paths*, "call it the Green Lion." I shall reveal this subject to you, as far as I dare, in the following song.

## The Learned
### SOPHIES FEAST.
<note>original: "Sophies Feast." "Sophie" here refers to a "Sophy" or a wise man/philosopher (from the Greek 'sophia' for wisdom), not a proper name.</note>

Whoever desires to deserve lasting and eternal fame,
Learn how to tame the Green Lion.
But before you can achieve this through the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy or the Great Work.</note>,
You must be eager to study in order to know him;
Trust me, it is not a task for a stupid fool,
Nor for one educated only in common schools.
I shall, therefore, portray him vividly,
Lest you leave this banquet hungry:
I title this song "The Learned Philosophers' Feast,"
Prepare yourself to arrive as a worthy guest:
Pay close attention to my words with an attentive mind,
Lest you feed on fantasies instead of food.
This horrid beast, which we call our "Lion,"
Has many other names, so that no man shall
Perceive the truth, unless God directs him,
And reflects a light upon his darkened mind.

<summary>Sir George Ripley introduces "The Sophies Feast," a poetic guide to alchemy, warning that the "Green Lion" (a key chemical secret) can only be understood through divine grace and rigorous study.</summary>

It is not because this subject consists
Of animal components (as anyone who wishes
May well conceive) that we therefore use
The name of "Beasts"; nor is it to deceive
The readers; whoever thinks so
Links himself with stupid fools.
But it is because of the transcendent force
It possesses, and because of the rawness of its source—
The likes of which can be seen nowhere else—
That it is named by them the *Green Lion* <note>original: "Lyon Green"; a famous alchemical term for the raw, acidic mineral state of mercury or antimony before purification.</note>.

Now listen, and I shall disclose to you
The Secret, which in times past has, like a rose,
Been hedged on every side with briars,
So that few could pluck it as their hearts desired.
There is a substance of metallic race—
If you view the matter—whose gloomy face
Would so scare a pretender <note>original: "Sophister"; here meaning a false philosopher or someone who reasons fallaciously</note> at first sight,
That he would never dare to approach it;
The visible form is very vile,
And so defiles metallic bodies,
That no one seeing it could be brought to think
That from it should spring bright Apollo's <note>original: "Phœbus"; the sun god, representing gold or the solar principle in alchemy.</note>

This spring two dreadful beasts have in
their keeping;

Which drive away rash searchers to their woe,

Runs down abundantly; then should the beams

It is metallic, and its color is black <note>original: "sable"</note>,
Intermixed with silver <note>original: "Argent"</note>, which in veins
Stains the black field with glittering branches.

In this Song, you have the *Green Lion* <note>original: "Lyon Green"; a common alchemical term for a potent solvent or a specific state of mercury/antimony</note> so described that I dare not say more—indeed, I cannot say more—unless I were to write down the recipe word-for-word, which both God and reason forbid.

## The Preface of Sir George Ripley.

<note>original: "summus experti, idque probatis rationibus & nihil invenimus præter solam unctuosam humiditatem penetrantem & tingentem &c."</note> "We have thoroughly tested this, and that with proven reasons, and we have found nothing except a single oily humidity that penetrates and tints," etc. And <term>Artephius</term> <note>A legendary alchemical figure, often claimed to have lived for over a thousand years by using the Elixir.</note> says there is no other subject in the world for this Art, naming it—although in a <term>philosophic</term> <note>original: "Philosophick"; referring to the specialized, often coded language used by alchemists.</note> manner—wonderfully subtly. I counsel you, along with <term>Ripley</term>, to learn to know this one thing which I have faithfully declared; and I know what I have declared experimentally to be true. He who understands me will have cause to thank God and me for the light I have given to Ripley's work. He who understands Ripley as I do will easily discern the truth.

*With the second, which is a vegetable humidity reviving what before was dead.*

Our second water, or <term>menstruum</term> <note>A solvent used in alchemy to dissolve solid bodies.</note>, or fire, is our <term>elixir</term>, which is a boiling down of our materials, or a drawing forth of the <term>tincture</term> <note>The essential color or soul of the metal that has the power to transmute other substances.</note> out of our dissolved bodies. This causes our dead body to rise and to spring forth in sprigs and branches, like the tender grass in the field during the spring; and this continues until

->Both Material Principles must be loosened.<-

Here your **Natures** are changed; they take hold of one another and become one inseparably. And then shall you know that your Compound is, by the living God, endowed with a vegetable Soul.

*Without which neither true Calcination* <note>original: Calcination; the process of reducing a metal to a fine powder or ash through heat.</note>
*can be done, nor true Dissolution.* <note>original: Dissolution; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note>

He who does not know the mystery of these two *Menstrues* <note>original: Menstrues; solvents or liquids used to extract or dissolve substances.</note> can never attain either the *Calcination* or the *Dissolution* of the Philosophers. The mystery of the first consists in activating your *Vinegar* <note>A symbolic term for an acidic solvent.</note> with the blood of our *Green Lion* <note>Often refers to raw mineral ore or antimony.</note> and the soul of the *Fiery Dragon* <note>Often refers to saltpeter or a volatile sulfurous substance.</note>. This is achieved by seven *Eagles* <note>Symbolic for seven cycles of distillation.</note>, which are seven distillations <note>original: Cohobations; the repeated distillation of a liquid from its own solid residue.</note> and purifications <note>original: Depurations</note> of your feminine *Sperm* <note>The receptive, liquid principle in the work.</note>, until it conceives a spiritual seed, or true natural heat, to animate your young *King* <note>The developing gold or the "Philosopher's Stone" in its early stage.</note>.

The mystery of the second *Menstrue* consists in the true proportion of your first Water with its own Body, and the administration of true external heat. By this heat, the combat between the *Eagles* and the *Lion* may be stirred up; thus shall the duel be ended, the *Lion* torn into pieces, and the carrion of its *Carcass* shall kill...

### The Preface of Sir George Ripley:

Kill the "Eagles" <note>In alchemy, "Eagles" often refer to the volatile vapors or the sublimation of mercury</note>; and out of these atoms, the second Water shall be made apparent by dissolution.

#### Regarding the third Humidity, which is most permanent:

The third <term>Menstrue</term> <note>original: "Menstrue"; a specialized alchemical solvent or catalyst used to dissolve metals</note> is called the "second Water" by <term>Artephius</term> <note>A famous 12th-century alchemical author</note>, because he joins our second water together with the first. However, in the sections where he describes the three Fires in detail, he then distinguishes three separate <term>Menstruums</term>.

Many philosophers have used a similar approach in the description of their operations. Some omit the first stage, or at least confuse it with the second, for the purpose of further obscuring the Art from the uninitiated.

But we have (beyond what any others have performed until now) particularly insisted upon the three in order. We have taken more pains in the discovery of the first because the wise Ancients took such great pains to conceal that one the most. After that, we made an orderly progression to the second, which we have handled in a similar fashion. This being performed, we now address ourselves to the third.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of chemical conjunction and the properties of the "permanent water" used in the alchemical process.</meta>

This is called by *Ripley* <note>George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), a famous English alchemist and author of *The Compound of Alchymy*.</note> a most permanent humidity. Note, by the way, that the first water is likewise called a permanent water by authors. However, take notice that there is a different reason for each name. 

First of all, all *mercury* is "permanent water"; that is, its parts have no heterogeneity <note>original: "Heterogeneity"; meaning the parts are uniform and not composed of different, separable substances.</note>. They will not leave one another during the test of the fire. Instead, either the whole substance flies away and is inconstant, or else the whole substance remains and is constant in the trial of *Vulcan* <note>original: "Vulcan"; the Roman god of fire, used here as a metaphor for the alchemist's furnace.</note>. Our first *menstruum* <note>term: "menstruum"; a solvent used in alchemy to dissolve solids.</note> acts in this same way. In this regard, our *mercury* and *common mercury* agree, in addition to having the same material identity; it is only the "form" <note>In Aristotelian philosophy, "form" is the essential nature or organizing principle of a thing that distinguishes it from other things made of the same matter.</note> that distinguishes them. 

But in the next place, our water is permanent with the "body" <note>The "body" usually refers to the solid metallic substance, such as gold or silver, being acted upon by the solvent.</note>, which *common mercury* is not. That is to say, through the process of digestion <note>term: "digestion"; the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat.</note>, our water unites with the body rather than just sticking to it. Consequently, both together make one single individual substance <note>original: "Individuum"</note>, which is achieved by our secret conjunction. 

Lastly, when the body is reduced by our water in this manner, it finally results in the four elements being united within this water. After the processes of *putrefaction* <note>term: "putrefaction"; the stage where the substance breaks down and turns black.</note> and *purification*, this leads to the final, most praiseworthy *four-fold conjunction* <note>original: "Tetraptive Conjunction"; referring to the perfect union of the four elements: earth, air, fire, and water.</note>,

<summary>The author explains the concept of "permanent water," distinguishing between common mercury and the alchemical solvent that permanently unites with a metallic body. This union eventually leads to the perfect integration of the four elements after the stage of putrefaction.</summary>

Know then, that if you are a "Son of Art" <note>A traditional term for a practitioner of alchemy.</note>, once you have arrived at this point, you are far from being at the end of your search. Unless you make **Gold** your final object—and if you do, you shall never reach this state—you have only just entered the mystical school of the hidden wonders of God. In this school, you may see new miracles every day if you are studious and desirous of knowledge, as all *Adepts* <note>original: "Adepti"; those who have attained the secret of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> are. They prize skill above any earthly thing; therefore, they refuse honor and pomp, retiring only to behold God and His works in this admirable looking-glass of the most hidden mysteries of nature.

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

Mercury is called "Vegetable" because it flies and returns, and causes the Earth to sprout and bud with tender branches. These two—the base oil or *Sulphur*, and the vegetable *Menstruum* <note>original: "Menstrue"; a solvent used in alchemy to extract the essence of a substance</note>—at last make one entire, indivisible substance. This substance is of an hermaphroditical quality: the Man is the Woman, and the Woman is the Man; the *Sulphur* is the *Mercury*, and the *Mercury* is the *Sulphur*. 

In this respect, the Earth flies as if it were Heaven, and at length the Heaven will precipitate in the form of Earth. Then our work is at an end. There is the harvest in the South, when the glorified Soul has become a spiritual Body, which is called our Stone of Paradise.

Go to the apothecaries, and you shall set it in a crucible over the fire, in such a heat that the *Mercury* <note>Quicksilver</note> may not evaporate as smoke. You may then project this mass upon any inferior metal, and it will transmute it into the most perfect form.

<meta>This page continues the preface of Sir George Ripley, discussing the dual nature of the Philosopher's Stone as both a source of wealth and a universal medicine.</meta>

# The Preface of Sir George Ripley

Regarding the essential Mercury of our Stone, which is our first Water, I shall leave it undiscovered. Otherwise—if it could be explained without the danger of exposing the other secret to unworthy people—I would proclaim it with trumpets for the good of mankind.

As *Raymond* <note>Raymond Lully (c. 1232–1315), a major figure in medieval alchemy and logic.</note> said when he was old, this would be worth much more than gold.

This Oil or Quintessence <note>term: The "fifth essence" or purest part of a substance.</note>, this Balsam of Health, we therefore prize for its wonderful virtue. We value it not so much because it allows one to avoid poverty—that great snare—but for its wonderful, prevailing excellence against all diseases of both body and mind. This is witnessed by *Lully*, *Trevisan*, *Artephius*, and that excellent book titled *The Golden Hour* <note>original: "Aurea Hora"</note>, or *The Rising Dawn* <note>original: "Confurgens Aurora," a famous 15th-century alchemical manuscript.</note>.

<summary>Sir George Ripley explains his decision to keep the secret of the "Essential Mercury" hidden from the unworthy while praising the alchemical Quintessence for its medicinal power to heal both body and mind.</summary>
<keywords>George Ripley, Raymond Lully, Bernard Trevisan, Artephius, Mercury, Quintessence, Balsam of Health, Alchemy, Universal Medicine</keywords>

--- Page 160 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the miraculous life-extending properties of the Philosopher's Stone and the accessibility of the Alchemical Art.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>An Exposition upon</header>

For when he was near death because of old age,
He made from it *Potable Gold* <note>original: "Aurum Potabile"; a medicinal liquid gold believed to grant longevity</note>,
Which revived him, as people could see.

By virtue of this <term>Quintessence</term> <note>The "fifth essence" or purest substance in alchemy, beyond the four elements</note>, Artephius testifies that he lived for more than a thousand years. Flamel also records of it that it triumphs over all the miseries of the world. *Johannes de Laznioro* is even bolder, and says that if a man should taste but a single grain of it while in the agony of death, all mortal <term>Pestilence</term> <note>Infectious disease or plague</note> would depart from him.

Since this knowledge is so wonderfully profitable—being the very looking-glass of Nature, the antidote against poverty and sickness, and consequently the "cut-throat" of covetousness, pride, ambition, and similar sordid emotions—who would not bestow a little time in the inquiry of it? In this matter, let me assure you in the very words of *Trevisan* <note>Bernard Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist</note>: the Art is so plain that if it were described nakedly, it would be held in contempt. And yet, in common <term>Mechanics</term> <note>Manual trades or crafts</note>, how frequent is it for men to serve seven, eight,

--- Page 161 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface by Sir George Ripley regarding the difficulty and accessibility of the alchemical art.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>The Preface of Sir George Ripley</header>

eight, or even ten years of apprenticeship to attain them? And in some people who are a little more clever, how frequently are considerable sums of money paid in addition to their labor? Yet in this noble Art—which so far excels all human sciences as the Sun excels a candle—who will employ himself with diligence? None, unless it be some money-minded fools <note>original: "Sots"</note> who seek only for riches; and yet, how soon are even they weary? So it is that few or none persist in their inquiry, except for a few roguish deceivers <note>original: "Sophisters"</note> who live by cheating <note>original: "Cozening"</note>. Because of such people, the Art is scandalized and spoken of poorly.

Yet trust me, for I speak from experience: the Art is both true and easy. Indeed, it is so easy that if you saw the experiment yourself, you could not believe it. I did not make five wrong experiments in it before I found the truth, although in some particular practical applications <term>Encheireis</term> <note>original: "Encheireis," a Greek term referring to the manual treatment or "hand-work" of a laboratory process</note>, I erred often. Yet even then, I knew in my error that I was a Master; and in less than two full years and a half, from being a common ignorant person <term>Ignoramus</term> <note>original: "vulgar Ignoramus"</note>, I became a true expert <term>Adept</term> <note>A person who has successfully discovered the "Philosopher's Stone"</note> and possessed the Secret through the goodness of God. And that this is true, there are those alive who can bear witness.

<summary>Sir George Ripley defends the validity of alchemy, arguing that while it is often dismissed due to the failures of greedy or dishonest men, it is a true and accessible science that he mastered in less than three years.</summary>

bear witness to this my writing, which I penned for the sake of the **Ingenious** <note>original: "Ingenious"; here meaning those possessing natural talent or intellectual curiosity</note>, that they may have **Ariadne's thread** <note>In Greek mythology, the thread used to navigate the Labyrinth; here, a metaphor for a guiding clue through complex alchemical secrets</note> stretched out to guide them. So, with the blessing of God, they shall by their own experience see and know that I wrote nothing but what the light of experience (far beyond all imaginary conjectures) has taught me to be true.

And now my Muse, let it not seem irksome
To you of Nature's **Mysteries** <note>The hidden inner workings of the natural world</note> to sing,
Those hidden mysteries which many deem
To bring nothing but delusions with them.
This is the opinion of the **rude vulgar** <note>The uneducated or common masses</note>,
To whom there is hardly any **selcouth** <note>original: "felcouth"; an archaic term for strange, rare, or marvelous</note> thing
But what seems a **juggling trick** <note>A deceptive sleight of hand</note> that would delude
Their fancies with an empty wondering;
Therefore against it they with thundering words do ring.

There is a fiery **Stone of Paradise**,
So called because of its celestial **hem** <note>Likely referring to its outer edge, appearance, or divine origin</note>,
Named in ancient years by wise **Sages**
**Elixir**, made of Earth and Heaven new,
Ana—

## The Preface of Sir George Ripley

Mixed in equal proportions <note>original: "Anatically mixt"</note>; strange to relate,
Sought for by many, but found out by few;
Above the changes of Nature, and by fate
Immortal, appearing like a fixed Body,
Whose penetrating power proves it to be a true Spirit.

Its property is as the Sages told:
To transmute six imperfect metals (which before would burn)
Into the most perfect Gold,
And to turn five into the finest Silver,
Not equaled by metals from the mine;
While some seek this, they madly seem to kick
Against the pricks <note>A reference to Acts 9:5, meaning to struggle uselessly against fate or truth</note>, which proves in the end to be a bad design,
And gives them cause to mourn sadly at length,
And to bewail their destiny, like men forlorn.

For why? They do not well consider beforehand
The nature of the thing they wish to attain;
Only the thirst for gain animates
These gold-adoring wretches, who maintain
Their mad expenses with many a cursed lie,

<summary>Sir George Ripley describes the miraculous transmutative powers of the Philosopher's Stone and warns against greedy "gold-adoring" seekers who fail to understand its true nature.</summary>

Nor from false perjury will they refrain:
Thus they allure fools by flattery,
To trust their foolishness <note>original: "dotage"</note> for the hope of gain;
This lasts so long, until they both remain in the suds <note>An archaic idiom meaning to be left in a state of helpless difficulty or ruin.</note>.

Then Covetousness wrangles with Deceit,
And curses him for all his ignorant <note>original: "lewd"</note> expense;
The other, being conscious of his cheat,
With subtle words makes a sly defense.
But what is past can never be recalled;
This grieves the Miser <note>original: "Churl"</note>, who vows for that offense
He will have the fraud's <note>original: "Juggler's"</note> future pranks stopped <note>original: "forestalled"</note>,
And throws him in jail <note>original: "Gaol"</note>. Oh, monstrous change! From there,
The great "Stone-maker" <note>A sarcastic reference to the fraudulent alchemist who claimed he could create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> begs for peace through a window grate.

Of Truth, let him avoid <note>original: "eschew"</note> such deceivers <note>original: "Sophisters"</note>,
And if he will only be advised by me,
I will warn him of false ways and show him the true.
Beg humbly of your God to be your guide,

### Next, learn to know the three different Kingdoms
*In which all earthly <note>original: "sublunary," meaning everything beneath the moon</note> things are created;*
*Reveal the hidden Bodies, whose status*
*Is noble, though they are found in very lowly places;*
*In this respect, they bear the names of the Planets,*
*And they may be brought to such a power <note>original: "Virtue"</note>*
*Which may fulfill the hardworking Chemist's goals,*
*And achieve that which will surpass the imagination*
*Of the uneducated masses <note>original: "rude Vulgar"</note>, who are taught only by their basic senses.*

*From which, if you are careful, you may discover*
*What substance they choose for their primary Material;*
*You may also learn of their hidden Agent:*

They tell you also what things you should refuse,

And she who is Queen-Regent of the place,
Is called Nature; she sits and rules
In Jehovah's <note>The Hebrew name for God.</note> stead, for she, next under God,

This round Castle is invincible <note>original: "Inexpugnable"</note> by force,

It stands immortal forever, so that no one is able
To conquer it by force, even if fraud
Concurs with it; its standing is so firmly stable,
There is no way to storm it, even if we could recall

And yet, withal, its beauty is so lovely.

It has twelve gates, and yet they are but
one,
And these twelve gates have only one
lock,
So that you either open all or none;
Nor can you force it open with a knock,
For violence cannot break it apart;
The outcome mocks those who attempt it.
Indeed, even if you could call down the dreadful
thunder,
It would be of no use; whoever shall employ
His wealth <note>original: "stock"</note> in such a way, may eventually walk in a
threadbare cloak. <note>A warning that attempting to force the alchemical process through violent heat or improper methods leads only to poverty.</note>

And create it, then I will call you a Master.

### Sir GEORGE RIPLEY'S
# Compound of *Alchemy*
### Expounded by a Son of ART. <note>The "Son of Art" is a traditional pseudonym for an anonymous practitioner of alchemy.</note>

---

### The first Gate opened,
Which is
# CALCINATION.

*Calcination is the Purification <note>original: "Purgation"</note> of our Stone.*

WE have led you, as it were, by the hand through many a waste field, and many a desert and thicket; and now, lift up your eyes and behold where you are. Welcome, my friend, into the Garden of the Philosophers. Here you may behold (like a landscape) a very glorious castle, walled about with a very high wall, and twelve gates, one following another, hindering your entrance and possession.

Then I lifted up my eyes, and behold, I saw Nature as a Queen gloriously adorned, sitting upon her Throne. In her hand was a beautiful book called *Philosophy Restored to its Primitive Purity*. When I approached any gate or door, they immediately opened of their own accord (as though they were moved by a sensitive spirit). One who has it cannot do. She, therefore, whom you seek, has gone into her retired solitudes, and as a legacy has left you two great treasures: the treasure of riches, and the treasure of long life.

Then I said, "Ah Sir, what you tell me is nothing but an aggravation of my misery. I count all the wealth in this world as nothing more than a straw in comparison to the enjoyment of that most admirable Lady's presence. I would consider her service a greater happiness than if I were master of all the rest of the world. If I may not see her again, my life will be a burden to me; and of what use, then, will long life be?"

Thus I sat bemoaning myself, and I heard a shrill voice as if it were close by me. I looked suddenly, and behold, an unspeakable light appeared, in comparison to which the sun itself seemed dark. Close by me I saw a most secret place, and within it a secret room made of transparent <term>diaphanous</term> <note>original: "Diaphanous matter"; meaning clear or translucent, often referring to the "Philosophical Glass" or the purity of the matter.</note> matter, and round in shape. This ignorance of yours is in no way provoking to me, for in these affairs—though you are a man—you are but a child. I allow this liberty to all my sons while they are children: to speak and think as children."

That you see me naked with this King, know that this place and my Kingdom are in the State of Innocence, though we are by the Fall of Adam laid subject to Vanity. Until the final restoration from that Fall, I am forbidden to work anything of my own accord beyond the state of fading corruptibility, even though all things have an incorruptible Spirit. At the very entrance of the Tower, the darkness was so thick that it would not receive the light. They were not of the same nature <note>original: "homogenial"</note>, but were slightly separated by the rays of the light. The darkness was, as it were, condensed here and there into strange figures, such as birds, beasts, and creeping things of monstrous shapes. Because of this opposition, the rays of the light extended only a little way; the clearest light shot out in beams, while the darkness stood apart in clusters.

Judging it to be an evil omen, they abandoned their positions. Their eyes were so sensitive from the darkness and smoke that my candle overpowered them; they could not endure its luster, and so, crying out, they ran away. # Sir George Ripley's First Gate

### With Sulphurs

I found one furnace most curiously built, in which all degrees of heat imaginable by the art of man might be kept with one fire. In this furnace was set a multitude of curious glasses <note>original: "Glasses"; refers to glass laboratory vessels like flasks or retorts</note>, in which were several matters: some digesting, others subliming, others distilling, and others calcining <note>These are the four primary alchemical stages of refinement: slow heating, vaporization, purification by steam, and reduction to powder through high heat</note>. 

Around the glasses and the furnace was written this quote from *Geber*: "By God, Sulphur is all that illuminating light which is above the earth" <note>original Latin: "Per Deum Sulphur est omne illud illuminans quod est supra terram." Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, a foundational figure in alchemy.</note>. By this I knew that Sulphur was the subject on which they worked. Indeed, it was done with such great cunning that I could not help but admire the ingenuity of the men. 

Knowing that what a man prizes (even if it is a trifle) would be an injury to spoil or destroy, I did not meddle with any glass. Indeed, there were laborers there who did not flee because they knew nothing, but only worked as they were directed. # Sir George Ripley's First Gate. 131

<margin>The reason for their error.</margin>
I wish to know the reason for their error, so that I may know how to avoid the same.

For by such <term>Calcination</term> <note>original: "calcination"; here referring to the harsh reduction of a substance to dust by fire</note> their bodies are spent,
Which diminishes the moisture of our <term>Stone</term>;
Therefore, when bodies are burned to powder,
As dry as the ashes of a tree or bone,
We will have nothing to do with such <term>Calxes</term> <note>original: "calxes"; the powdery metallic oxide remaining after heating</note>:
For we multiply the radical moisture,
Diminishing none at all during our Calcining.

Then she said: Besides the fact that they do not work on the true <term>Matter</term>, they do not work in the right way, which are two most desperate errors. For our work is to make a substance fluid, penetrating, and entering, so that it may have ingress into imperfect <term>Metals</term>. For this cause, we preserve <term>humidity</term> <note>original: "humidity"; the "mercurial" or life-giving moisture of the substance</note>, without which our <term>Stone</term> cannot be <term>penetrative</term>. 

So then, instead of purifying the crude and ripening what is raw by these <term>Calcinations</term>, the tender <term>Soul</term> is put to flight, and the <term>crudities</term> <note>original: "crudities"; the raw, impure elements of the metal</note> are more strongly vitrified <note>original: "vitrified"; turned into a glass-like state that cannot be further altered</note>, so that all hope of fruit is wholly taken away by this means. For take this as a rule: whatever, either by the violence of <term>Fire</term>...

### Sir George Ripley's *First* Gate

The Water-bearer's Daughter—of which Water-bearer I told you that his body, his pitcher, and the water in it are all one—and his daughter was the Queen who arose out of the water, in which was seen a burning lamp. Do not wonder that a Queen should spring from a Water-bearer's loins, for the King is also his son, and he is greater than both. 

For know, that in this place there is nothing so hard to obtain as Water, which cannot be brought except by him who has the keys of the whole kingdom. Take this then as a great secret: our Water-bearer is father to the King and Queen. The King, being of mature age, is at his own disposal and enjoys more riches than his father; but his father has the key to a closet <note>original: "Closet"; here meaning a private room or treasury</note> in which there are riches enough for everyone in the kingdom, to make every subject as rich as the King. 

However, only the King is to have the disposal of this wealth; yet he cannot have it in his possession until he marries his sister, who is invisible within the water of the pitcher. This sister of his is also his mother and his father, for she is one with the Water-bearer, the

The Water and the Pitcher, as has been said. By reason of his consanguinity <note>original: "Confanguinity"; meaning blood-relationship or shared essence</note>, the King embraces his sister very desirously, and through his embraces she appears as a Queen. Then the Water-bearer, his water, and his pitcher vanish, and the King and Queen remain alone. At length, both King and Queen are drowned after the immoderate use of sexual union <note>original: "Venery"</note> and violent sweating, weeping, and urinating. This sweat, these tears, and this urine create one Sea, in which swim two fishes without flesh and bones; these later dissolve and create one broth, which is called "permanent water" <note>original: "Water permanent"; a stable alchemical solvent</note>.

*He may wander forth like mist in the wind,*
*Never knowing where to settle with profit,*
*Because he does not understand our words correctly.*

--- Page 209 ---
<page-num>135</page-num>
<header>Sir George Ripley's First Gate</header>

Understanding the causes of things is essential; otherwise, every puff of argument from the Sophisters <note>original: "Sophisters"; refers to those who use clever but fallacious arguments to deceive</note> will toss you about, just as a feather is tossed in the air by a blast of wind. Our books are full of obscurity, and Philosophers write in dreadful metaphors and riddles for those who do not stand upon a firm foundation. Without that base, the texts are like a running stream that will carry the uninitiated headlong into despair and errors. They can never escape these errors until they understand our writings well enough to discern the true subject matter of our secrets; once that is known, the rest is not so difficult.

->Join like with like, as reason dictates,<-
->For every bud <note>original: "Burgeon"</note> answers to its own seed;<-
->Man begets man, and a beast a beast, I know,<-
->There is no need to treat further on this:<-
->But understand this point if you wish to succeed,<-
->Each thing is first Calcined <note>term: Calcination; the process of reducing a substance to a powder or ash through heat, here used metaphorically for the first step of the alchemical process</note> in its own kind;<-
->If you conceive this well, you shall find fruit therein.<-

Therefore, do not take one step further until you have learned this lesson: namely, to wed family with family <note>original: "Consanguinity with Consanguinity"; in alchemy, this refers to combining substances that share a common nature or "blood"</note>.

<summary>Sir George Ripley warns that without understanding the fundamental "subject matter" of alchemy, seekers will be misled by the riddles of philosophers, emphasizing the core principle that like must be joined with like.</summary>

--- Page 210 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's philosophical and alchemical instructions regarding the principles of generation and the necessity of working within the natural order of species.</meta>

<page-num>136</page-num>
<header>An Exposition upon</header>

<note>original: "guinity"</note> kinship, and consider well what it is you desire to produce; let your intention be guided by that goal. Take the final result you intend to achieve as the first principle of your work. This is according to Nature, and it is the true ground of all generation, for nothing engenders outside of its own kind. A man begets a man, and not a lion; nor does a lion beget a sheep. A rose does not produce a thorn, nor a nettle a gillyflower <note>A carnation or clove-scented pink</note>. If it were necessary, I could specifically demonstrate this throughout all vegetable, animal, and mineral bodies; however, it is such a plain truth that I need say no more. I leave it with you as the foundation stone on which you shall build whatever you intend. 

Attempt nothing outside of its own nature and kind, lest you harvest a delusion instead of the truth. Whatever you intend to increase by way of <term>multiplication</term> <note>The alchemical process of increasing the power or quantity of a substance</note>, attempt it only within its own kind. ## Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

If you know how to apply this doctrine in your operation as you ought, you will find great benefit in it, and a door will be opened by this to the discovery of greater mysteries.

And we make *unctuous <note>original: "unctuous," meaning oily or greasy</note> Calx*, black, white, and red.

And now the opportunity of this discourse leads me to handle our magical <term>Calxes</term> <note>Calx: a powder produced by heating a substance; in alchemy, it refers to the refined essence of a metal after "calcination."</note>. Know that we do <term>calcine</term> three times in our work. The general principle of this is that our Calxes are not burnt, dry powders, but are unctuous. For in them, we ennoble the <term>Sulphurs</term>, which are the basis of <term>Radical Humidity</term> <note>Radical Humidity: the essential, life-giving moisture believed to be inherent in all matter.</note>, which <term>Raymund</term> <note>Refers to Ramon Lull (c. 1232–1315), a famous philosopher and reputed alchemist.</note> calls <term>Oils</term> and <term>Ointments</term>. 

So then, our Calxes are all of one kind and differ only in color and maturity. Our first is black of the blackest and is called <term>Saturn</term> <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the symbol for lead and the stage of "putrefaction" or decay.</note>. Our second is white of the whitest and is called <term>Luna</term> <note>Luna is the symbol for silver and the stage of purification.</note>. Our third is red of the reddest and is called <term>Sol</term> <note>Sol is the symbol for gold and the final stage of perfection.</note>.

### Of the three degrees of our Base being perfect.

These <term>Calxes</term> <note>original: "Calxes"; the powdery remains left after a substance has been heated or "burned" in a crucible</note> are the completion of so many <term>Circulations</term> <note>original: "Circulations"; the process of distilling a liquid and letting it condense and flow back into the original vessel</note>, and have specific degrees of heat allowed to each of them. Our first circulation confounds the Elements, our second renews the Air, and the third exalts the Fire; then is our <term>Stone</term> perfect.

#### Flexible as Wax, otherwise they are of no use.

Our <term>Calxes</term> thus graduated are distinguishable from all other calxes in the world. First, they are produced without any "laying on of hands" <note>meaning the process happens automatically within the sealed vessel without manual interference</note>. Secondly, they are distinguished by the exquisite subtlety of their parts—for they are finer than <term>Atoms of the Sun</term> <note>a poetic reference to dust motes visible in a sunbeam</note>—joined with an eminent humidity. Because of this moisture, to the astonishment of beholders, they flow like melted wax. The first calx flows in the glass vessel by the continuation of heat, as does the second, since they have not yet reached their final period of perfection. The second and third calxes will also flow when placed upon a plate of hot metal, or on a melted metal, or on

### Sir George Ripley's First Gate

<margin>Mercury heated so long until it is ready to fly.</margin>
By a truly long process, as the Philosophers <note>original: "Philosophers"; referring to historical alchemists</note> write, we take a year or more for our respite. For in less time, our calxes <note>original: "Calxes"; the powdery remains left after a metal has been roasted or oxidized</note> will not be made able to combine <note>original: "tyne"; to kindle or unite</note> with a tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; a medicinal or alchemical substance capable of transmuting or healing</note> that will not fade.

These final calxes represent the peak of artistic skill and nature's power in this matter, yet they require a considerable time to perfect. The first stage—the white—takes five months, and the red takes nine and a half months, which is the true duration. We allow one month for the preparation of our materials, and three months for fermentations and imbibitions <note>original: "Imbibitions"; the process of a solid absorbing a liquid</note>. Thus, our whole operation lasts over a year. 

Indeed, we do not expect an ordinary result, but a fire-abiding tincture which is unfading and incorruptible; such a thing cannot be expected in a short time. Yet, truly, the industry of the workman may move his work forward or set it back by a month, two, or three, accor-

That is seven *Eagles*, which are not added to the *Sun* until the third stage; by adding four more, you reach seven.

Therefore, for your true information, follow this rule: Let your solid material <term>Body</term> be very well refined <note>original: "subtilized"</note> and very pure (which is a significant matter—at least 24 carats). Mix this at first with twice as much of its Water, and grind it either on a clean glass or a marble mortar. Grind it thoroughly, as painters use to grind their pigments <note>original: "Colours"</note>, and do not take this lightly; for lack of an hour or even a half-hour's labor in your mixing <term>Amalgamation</term>, you may set your work back twenty or thirty days. For the more finely <meta>catchword: subtly</meta>

The more finely <note>original: "subtly"</note> the *amalgam* <term>Amalgama</term> is mixed, the more easily and quickly it dissolves into *mercury*, allowing it to be worked upon until the signs <note>In alchemy, "signs" are specific color changes or physical transformations that indicate the work is progressing correctly.</note> appear. When you have thoroughly <note>original: "soundly"</note> and well ground it, washed it very clean, and dried it very completely so that there is not the slightest moisture in it, observe the consistency <note>original: "temper"</note>.

If it is as pliable as paste, yet such that when you tilt it one way or the other, you see no water running to the lower side (which you may easily discern), it is a good and reliable consistency. However, if it is so hard and dry that it will not spread easily, it lacks moisture. Or, if that excessive fluid <term>hydropical water</term> <note>original: "Hydropical water"; a reference to dropsy or edema, used here to describe an unhealthy excess of liquid.</note> runs, as if within a skin, to the declining side of your *amalgam*, add more of your solid material <term>Body</term> to it until you no longer see that sign. 

Grind it thoroughly, as has been said; it is better to err on the side of dryness than on the side of excess moisture <note>original: "rather choose to lean to the other hand than to this"</note>. There is nothing more frustrating <note>original: "irksome"</note> to an alchemist <term>Artist</term> during his training <term>Scholarship</term> than to have to wait for his signs of progress beyond the expected time.

# An Exposition upon
...when they misinterpret <note>original: "misconstrue"</note> our meaning most perversely. For is it imaginable that those who are fools regarding Nature should be wise in our books, which are themselves testimonies of Nature?

In eggs, in vitriol <note>sulfuric acid or various sulfate minerals</note>, or in blood,
What riches do they think <note>original: "ween"</note> to find there?
If they understood philosophy,
They would not be so blind in their work
As to seek gold and silver from things not of their kind.
For just as fire is the principle of burning,
So the principle of gilding is gold, I know <note>original: "I wis"; certainly</note>.
If you therefore intend to make
Gold or silver by the craft of our philosophy,
Take neither eggs nor blood,
But gold and silver which, being naturally
And wisely calcined <note>original: "Calcined"; the process of heating a substance until it is reduced to a powder</note>—not merely by manual labor <note>refers to the "Philosophical" calcination rather than common, crude chemical heating</note>—
Will bring forth a new generation,
Increasing its kind just as every other thing does.

Some, I know, will treat my book as they have treated others: they will read into it their own fantastic <note>imaginary or irrational</note> processes which I never dreamed of, nor do they even exist in Nature. And whatever I write most plainly, they will allegorize and say it is true...
