<meta>This page continues the author's philosophical exploration of the origins of matter, explaining how the Creator's command established the boundaries between different kinds of existence, preventing the mixing of unrelated species.</meta>

<page-num>165</page-num>

# Sir G. Ripley's first Gate.

Creator, first impressed or sealed in the word *let it be done* <note>original: Latin *fiat*</note> and ratified in his command *let each thing produce according to its own kind* <note>original: Latin *producat unumquodque juxta speciem suam*</note>. Now, to apply this to our present purpose: in <term>Water</term> and <term>Light</term>, all things are the same <note>original: "agree"</note>, but in the specific way they are illuminated, they differ. This final decision <note>original: "determinative sentence"</note> of the Almighty established a great variety in the products of <term>Matter</term> and <term>Form</term>, which are general in themselves. Once established this way, nothing can pass from its own kind to mix with another kind without causing a product that shares qualities of both parents. Furthermore, a mixture cannot be made except within the same *genus* <note>a broad category or classification</note> or *species* <note>a specific group of organisms</note>. For example, an apple may be grafted onto a crab-apple tree, or a man might (though it is an abomination) mix with a beast (*by natural license* <note>original: Latin *licentia naturali*; meaning physical possibility despite moral prohibition</note>). But outside of their *genus* or *species*, nothing can mix.

There are also many particular exceptions among things within a single *genus*. For I know many trees that human skill <note>original: "the Art of man"</note> cannot graft into one another so that they will grow, even though they will grow well when grafted elsewhere. Similarly, a dog and a mouse cannot mix because they are so disproportionate to each other. But this is just a side note <note>original: "this by the by"</note>.

# An Exposition upon

Returning to our main point, we say that just as all things are given their specific nature by the will and power of God, so too, when that specific kind is destroyed, the <term>Form</term> <note>original: "Form"; in Aristotelian and alchemical philosophy, the "form" is the essential nature or "soul" of an object that makes it what it is</note> of that individual perishes (for no entire species can truly perish). Therefore, things may—both by Nature and by Art—return to their first stable material principle, which is **Water**. If Nature found this Water in a suitable place and impregnated it with a **metallic seed**, it might produce a **metallic sperm** or viscous substance. This could then be turned into a metal through <term>decoction</term> <note>the process of heating or "cooking" a substance to mature it</note> and provide a useful subject for our alchemical work.

*Whether in blood, eggs, hair, urine, or wine,*
*Or in common minerals dug out of the mine;*
*Yet must that element first be purified and separated,*
*And with the elements of perfect bodies be joined.* <note>original: "desponsate," meaning to be betrothed or united in marriage; a common alchemical metaphor for the chemical union of substances</note>

So then, if you are able—as you may be by using the liquor <term>Alcahest</term> <note>a hypothetical "universal solvent" capable of reducing any body to its primary matter</note>—to reduce a body (whatever it may be, whether eggs, or hair, or urine, or a <term>burning spirit</term> <note>original: "spirit ardent"; likely referring to distilled alcohol or a volatile chemical</note>, or any common mineral that does not naturally have a metallic nature)...

### Sir George Ripley's *First* Gate

The Stone is inspired and takes life in the air, and lives and shows the actions of life; and a living water or dew shall descend from the top of the glass onto the lower grounds and make them fruitful. Then the central fire, which was hidden in the earth and is now in the water, shall come forth and ascend with the water; in the form of air and vapor, it shall beautify your vessel with changing colors: citrine <note>a lemon-yellow color</note>, pale, bluish, and blackish. 

This is the fire of radical sulphur which, once it is stirred up, is like the Fiery Dragon and the *infernal fire* <note>original: "Ignis Infernalis"</note>. By this you may know that the heaven and the earth, the form and the matter, the male and the female are now beginning their conjunction <note>conjunction: the chemical and symbolic union of opposite principles</note>. 

When you see this sign, rejoice; for know that now your "bodies" <note>the metallic solids</note> are for the most part made "no bodies" <note>meaning they have been dissolved or spiritualized into a non-solid state</note>. If you work well, this will happen in 30 or 36 days.

<header>An Exposition upon</header>

*This done, go backwards turning your wheel again,*
*And into water turn your fire soon <note>original: "anon"</note>,*
*Air into earth, otherwise you labor in vain.*

Now know that all our three circulations <note>circulation: the process of a substance being evaporated and then condensed back into a liquid in a continuous cycle</note> are named this for good reason, for they truly move like a wheel. If you put a nail in a wheel and turn it, you shall see that the nail will ascend with one half of the turn and descend with the other half. When that happens, the circulation is complete, and you must then move on to another circulation.

Our circulations consist of solution <note>dissolving a solid into a liquid</note> and congelation <note>thickening or solidifying a liquid</note>, volatilization <note>turning a substance into vapor</note> and fixation <note>making a volatile substance stable and resistant to heat</note>, and opening and shutting. 

Once you have brought your "body" <note>the solid material in the vessel</note> to the highest degree of refinement <note>original: "subtiliation"</note> that the spirit can achieve through boiling <note>original: "decoction"</note>, then the spirit has finished its work and ceases to be active. At that point, the dissolved body begins to work according to its own nature; the spirit becomes passive, and the body becomes active. In this way, passive natures are made active, and active natures are made passive. This is the key of our Ma- <note>The text likely continues "Magistery," meaning the masterwork or the philosopher's stone</note>

...and the Mercury of the Water. Then, the combined Sulphur will overpower the combined humidity, rotting the substance into a powder as fine as atoms—the blackest of blacks. At that point, you will see a total mixing of the "seeds" <note>the essential elemental components of the metal</note> and the "death" of your compound.

This rotting will begin around the 42nd, 46th, or 50th day. The signs of it are as follows: no fumes will rise, but the matter will boil at the bottom of the glass flask like melted pitch <note>a thick, dark, resinous substance like tar</note>. It will boil and bubble, swelling and puffing in a black color, becoming blacker every day and showing various shifting colors of decay as it boils.

This will continue until the substance becomes so thick that it no longer boils, but instead grows hard and swells. However, its appearance will change frequently: sometimes appearing dry, and sometimes showing a little moisture with fresh bubbling, though still with no rising fumes. 

This stage will last about 46 days with no fumes rising at all. Thus, by about the 84th or 90th day after your materials began their continuous boiling <note>term: decoction; the process of heating or boiling a substance to extract its essence or change its state</note>, the process of Putrefaction <note>the "death" and decomposition of the material, which alchemists considered a necessary step before it could be purified and reborn</note> will be complete. Then, the stages of Sublimation or Circulation will begin again, which will result in the "white Dove" <note>a symbol for the *albedo* or white stage, representing the purification and spiritualization of the matter</note> after another 46 or 50 days.

<meta>This page continues the description of the "nigredo" (blackness) stage of the alchemical process, specifically explaining why the substance is compared to a toad and how it eventually thickens into a fine powder.</meta>

<page-num>177</page-num>

<header># Sir George Ripley’s First Gate</header>

...a tree afterward, beautiful and glorious with sprigs and branches and changing colors.

And indeed, the philosophers have taken this liberty to call it whatever they please. They call it their "Toad" which crawls on the ground and feeds upon the slime of the earth; this is because before it turns completely black, it can resemble the colors of a toad, as well as its appearance—puffing, swelling, and rugged with lumps <note>original: bunches</note>, blisters, and knobs.

Others call it a Spirit killed with its own deadly poison—that is, *Mercury dissolving Gold*. In this dissolved body (which then appears to be a spirit), there is a hidden ferment which can solidify <note>original: recongeal</note> it again. This fermenting power <note>original: virtue</note> is what thickens <note>original: coagulate</note> the water. To the wonder of those watching, the substance that previously grew thinner and thinner begins to thicken after forty days, until it becomes a dust or powder like microscopic <note>original: impalpable</note> atoms.

...wheel, which runs one half of its cycle directly backward to its first progress. You sublime <note>sublimation: the process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note> for so long until the Body is made as volatile <note>volatile: easily evaporated at normal temperatures</note> as it can be; this is the activity of the Spirit. Then you congeal the substance for so long until it all appears like atoms, and then your "bodily virtue" is active and your Spirit is passive. Then your Spirit begins to be active again, and your Compound—which was apparently stable <note>original: fixed</note>—softens and liquefies <note>original: relents</note> again and distills as before, until it reaches its peak of volatility once more, which is again a Separation. 

Then a fourfold conjunction <note>original: Conjunction Tetraptive; likely referring to the union of the four elements</note> is celebrated, and from that time everything rises and falls together. There is such a perfect union that a rapid vapor no longer exhales and descends upon the physical mass <note>original: bodily Moles</note> as it did at first. Instead, the whole substance rises like a glorious tree with branches; it is not merely vaporized to the top of the vessel, but sprouts up like the delicate frost on a clear morning, which falls and rises until everything becomes an incredibly fine <note>original: impalpable</note> powder. 

So then, after Calcination <note>heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to powder</note> comes another Solution <note>the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid</note>, and that divides the **Azoth** <note>the universal solvent or essential mercury of the philosophers</note> from the **Laton** <note>the base or impure metallic body that must be purified</note>, creating a distilling Separation in which the **Azoth** wash—

In this way, a third cycle of Solution, Sublimation, and Calcination is performed to create the **red Elixir**, which is the "Sabbath" <note>the day of rest or completion</note> of both Nature and Art. Once you arrive at this point, no further progress can be made without a new "Marriage" <note>the chemical union of substances</note>, whether through a **ferment** <note>a substance added to trigger a transformative change</note> or by some other means, according to the laws of Nature and Art. Thus, our entire work consists of three **rotations** <note>cycles</note>, and every rotation has three parts <note>original: Members</note>: Solution, Sublimation, and Calcination. 

The first "Solution" is called **inceration** <note>the process of softening a substance to a wax-like consistency</note>, and reduction or liquefaction; the second is properly called Solution; and the third is again called inceration. The first "Sublimation" is called distillation, ascension, and descension; the second is called separation and **ablution** <note>purification by washing or cleansing</note>; the third is called exaltation and sublimation. <header>Sir George Ripley's *First* Gate.</header>

The first **Calcination** <note>the process of heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or change its properties</note> is called Calcination, **tripartite** <note>original: Triptative; consisting of three parts</note> **Conjunction** <note>union of substances</note>, **Putrefaction** <note>the stage of decay or breakdown that must precede new growth</note>, and so on. The second is **Congelation** <note>the thickening or solidification of a liquid</note>, **Albification** <note>the process of making white</note>, and **Fixation** <note>making a substance stable in fire so it does not evaporate</note>. The third is **Illumination** <note>the stage of brightening or reaching the red state</note>, and so forth.

I only remind you that in your first Calcination, you attain complete Putrefaction; in the second, you attain the complete **white Elixir**; and in the third, the complete **Red Elixir**. 

I state this beforehand to prevent you from being deceived, so that you do not think you must have a Calcination first, then a **Dissolution** <note>melting or dissolving</note> next, a **Separation** third, a Conjunction fourth, a Putrefaction fifth, and so forth. No, truly; when you first put your materials into the vessel, on the first day of your operation, you apply a fire in which your compound boils, swells, and puffs up visibly. Drops run down in veins from the curved top <note>original: convex</note> of your glass vessel. For in this **Mercury** <note>the alchemical solvent or "Water"</note>, your **Gold** <note>the solid philosophical substance or "Seed"</note> will—beyond the nature of any other mercury—flow in the fire as if the whole substance were mercury. It will boil visibly, and this must never cease, not even for a moment, for any interruption causes imminent damage.

In the first days of your boiling, which is accompanied by a constant ascent and return of fumes, your compound grows

## Sir George Ripley’s First Gate

Pay close attention now, for I will not provide such a detailed explanation <note>original: "particular System"</note> of the work again in all my writings. When the process of **calcination** <term>the reduction of a substance to powder or ash through prolonged heat</term> is fully complete, the parts will begin to liquefy together once more. You will then see vapors begin to rise again, appearing first like smoke. Later, these will return as drops condensing on the sides of the vessel, which, believe me, is a magnificent sight. During this stage <note>original: "Operation"</note>, as the blackness gradually wears away, such colors will appear that you cannot even imagine. You will be so tempted to satisfy your eyes by watching it that you will steal time from your natural rest <note>original: "Nature's due"</note> when you should be sleeping.

This **circulation** <term>the continuous cycle of evaporation and condensation within a sealed glass vessel</term>—with its infinite variety of colors—will last between 20 and 30 days. Then, you will see your **matter** <note>the alchemical substance being transformed</note> appear quite white. It will then grow whiter and whiter until it becomes like a glittering sword in the sunbeams. Trust me, for I have seen this shining, sparkling white. Yet it will remain "quick" <note>meaning "alive" or fluid; also a play on the word "quicksilver"</note> like a most glorious, heaven-born **Mercury** <note>here referring to "Philosophical Mercury," a purified state of the matter, rather than common liquid metal</note>, which is a subject of wonders.

Then these fumes will begin to cease, and

### Sir G. Ripley's First Gate

...and its qualities, between which a Spirit of Life intervenes. This spirit carries them up and down like a wheel that turns until it returns to the place where it began. Thus, gold is your base or foundation—the center to which all your operations return and in which they rest. These operations are nothing but circulations of their own kind, and these circulations are incessantly carried along by the never-ceasing action of the fire. Even a small interruption would significantly delay the process, and letting the heat go out would extinguish it beyond recovery. If anyone were to ask us what the natural operation of the **Stone** is, we would answer: it is the making of active natures passive, and passive natures active, by continual boiling <term>original: "decoction"</term>. One without the other has no joy,
Both are descended from one.
Twice forty days <note>eighty days</note> come and go,
To which twice five <note>ten days</note> are added;
These produce a perfect Crow <note>original: "Crow," a symbol for the *Nigredo* or black stage of alchemy, representing the "death" or decomposition of the matter</note>,
Whose blackness cheers hearts that were weary <note>original: "fadded," meaning faded, faint, or discouraged</note>.
Twice fifteen more <note>thirty days</note> produce a Dove <note>original: "Dove," a symbol for the *Albedo* or white stage, representing purification and the "White Work"</note>,
Whose wings are bright and tender;
Twice ten more <note>twenty days</note> make the Soul above
To need no protection from the fire.
For Soul and Body so combine,
With the Spirit interceding,
To give the Tincture <term>tincture: a potent alchemical substance capable of transforming or "dyeing" base metals into silver or gold</term> of fine silver,
As the Soul leads the Body.
Also, it adds such fixity <term>fixity: the quality of being "fixed," meaning the substance can endure intense heat without evaporating</term>,
Prevailing against the flames,
Which may make the <term>Chymist</term> <note>original: "Chymist," here referring to a true practitioner of alchemy</note> completely glad,
While the <term>Sophister</term> <note>original: "Sophister," a false or deceptive practitioner who uses flawed logic or fraudulent methods</note> continues to fail.
He who seeks in fantasies to find
Our Art, which is so well concealed,
Does not duly weigh in his mind
That it is a sealed fountain,
Which only one thing can unlock;
Learn to know this one thing,
Lest you should mock the same outcome,
For these lines show you that very thing.

## The Second Gate Opened,
### Which is
# DISSOLUTION.

I will now speak a word or two about Dissolution,
Which reveals what was formerly <note>original: "erst"</note> hidden from sight,
And also makes those things thin <note>original: "intenuate"</note> that were thick,
By the power of our first solvent <term>menstrue: an alchemical liquid or "solvent" used to dissolve solid matter</term>, clear and bright;
In this solvent, our bodies are eclipsed of light,
And refined <note>original: "subtilate"</note> from their hard and dry compaction,
Naturally returning <note>original: "kindly retrograde"</note> into their own first matter <term>first matter: "materia prima," the original, unformed substance from which all things are created</term>.

Having completed the chapter on Calcination <term>calcination: the first stage of the alchemical process, where a substance is heated until it is reduced to a powder</term>, I now come to discuss Dissolution <term>dissolution: the second stage of the Great Work, where the solid material is dissolved into a liquid state</term>. As I mentioned before, this is the very beginning of the spirit's activity <note>original: "Spirits activity," referring to the movement and operation of the volatile essence within the alchemical vessel</note>, and it represents the first half of the...

<header>An Exposition upon</header>

...the Wheel <note>The "wheel" refers to the continuous cycle of evaporation and condensation (refluxing) within the alchemical vessel.</note> which raises the Spirit and brings down the Body; the second operation has a contrary effect, for it makes the Body active and the Spirit passive. Thus, <term>Calcination</term> conceals the inner depths <note>original: "profundity"</note> of the Body, which <term>Solution</term> then reveals. It is, therefore, nothing more than the boiling of hard and dry materials <note>original: "Bodies"</note> in our <term>Mercury</term> <note>Mercury: in alchemy, this is not just the metal, but a "philosophical" solvent capable of dissolving metals.</note> over a suitable fire, until they are dissolved and liquefied <note>original: "made thin"</note>. 

Then, the same fire causes them to rise <note>original: "fly," referring to evaporation</note>; as they rise, they condense and return as drops onto the solid material below, moistening it. This process is <term>Solution</term> and <term>Sublimation</term> <note>Sublimation: usually the process of turning a solid directly into a gas, but here used to describe the rising and falling of vapors in a "circular" distillation.</note> occurring together. The "Water" <note>The distilled liquid or solvent.</note> circulates over the solid matter and softens it; by repeatedly returning as a liquid, it eventually brings the matter back to its own natural state of moisture. 

In this <term>Resolution</term> <note>The breaking down of a substance into its component parts.</note>, according to the alchemist <term>Artephius</term> <note>Artephius: a famous medieval alchemical author, often cited as an authority on the longevity granted by the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, the <term>Sun</term> <note>In alchemy, the Sun represents Gold or the active male principle.</note> loses its color and is darkened, and the <term>Moon</term> <note>The Moon represents Silver or the passive female principle.</note> does not give her light. This happens because all things are returned to their confused <term>Chaos</term>, or <term>First Matter</term>, in which the elements and their qualities are all mixed together.

### Sir George Ripley's Second Gate

They are one in gender, and two in number,
Whose Father is the **Sun**, and the **Moon** the Mother;
The mover is **Mercury**: these and no more are
Our **Magnesia** <note>Magnesia: in alchemy, this does not refer to the element magnesium, but to the "First Matter" or the white, crude substance from which the Stone is prepared.</note>, our **Adrop** <note>Adrop: an alchemical term for the "lead of the philosophers," the raw, unrefined material of the work.</note>, and no other
Things are here, but only **Sister** and **Brother**:
That is to say, **Agent** <note>Agent: the active, masculine principle or "seed" that initiates change.</note> and **Patient** <note>Patient: the receptive, feminine material that undergoes change.</note>,
**Sulphur** and **Mercury** <note>Sulphur and Mercury: the two primary principles in alchemical theory; Sulphur represents fixity and heat, while Mercury represents fluidity and moisture.</note> both essential to our purpose.

The cause of this is the **homogeneity** <note>homogeneity: having the same nature or origin.</note> of the **matter**, in which they agree in essence, despite the difference in sex between them. They exist in the **Glass** <note>Glass: the alchemical vessel or "philosopher's egg."</note> as Male and Female; one being more mature, and consequently more active (namely, the **Sun**, who is therefore the Father), and the other more crude <note>original: "crude," meaning raw, unrefined, or immature.</note> in comparison to the **Sun**, and so more passive—that is, the **Moon**, which is therefore the Mother of our **Stone**. This Mother is our **Mercury** (which is called the **Moon** because of its great difference from any ordinary mercury) with its true internal **Sulphur**, which is hidden beneath its **mercurial form**.

The <term>Sulphur</term> <note>Sulphur: In alchemy, the active, combustible principle, often associated with the "soul" of a metal and its internal heat.</note>, or Fire of the Gold—which is the Fire of Nature—and the Sulphur of the Water embrace one another. These two together create an "unnatural fire" <note>original: "unnatural Fire." Also known as *ignis innaturalis*, this refers to a chemical or corrosive heat that dissolves solid matter without using a physical flame.</note>. In this state, the moisture <note>original: "Humidity"</note> becomes visible, while the Sulphur remains hidden from the eye, manifesting only through its effects. These effects are that it burns, destroys, and conquers the <term>Bodies</term> <note>Bodies: The solid, physical forms of the metals being transformed.</note> in a way that common fire never could, turning them from solid matter into a fume of mineral vapor. 

In this operation, the elements are mingled together <note>original: "confused"</note> to create our <term>Chaos</term> <note>Chaos: The "massa confusa," a state of primal matter where the elements are mixed and undifferentiated before being purified.</note>, which is void and dark. At this stage, the "lights of the world" are eclipsed; the Sun is darkened, and the Moon does not show its light. Because of the abundance of moisture and the absence of light in these combined substances <note>original: "Compositions"</note>, we call this stage "Winter," "Night," and the "North Latitude" of our <term>Stone</term> <note>Stone: The Philosopher’s Stone, the final perfected substance of the Great Work.</note>.

## Sir G. Ripley's second Gate.

But Mineral, specifically *Mercury* which wets no hand, nor clings to anything but that which is of its own substance and essence.

So it is that in our Work, our two Principals <note>Principals: The primary substances or elements used in the alchemical process, usually referring to sulfur and mercury.</note> do not act according to their individual dispositions, but in conjunction. The one, says the Philosopher <note>original: "saith the Philosopher"; likely referring to an authority like Hermes Trismegistus or Aristotle.</note>, does not die without its brother. Therefore, when you calcine <note>calcine: To heat a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or ash.</note> the Earth, you do, in it and with it, calcine the Water. In this process, the souls of both are tied together so that they may serve the wise philosophers. 

Therefore, let all your study be to unite these Natures. This you can never do unless you first separate their souls by sublimation <note>sublimation: The process of purifying a substance by heating it into vapor and then condensing it back into a solid.</note>, and afterward unite them in blackness <note>blackness: Also known as the *nigredo*, this is the first major stage of the alchemical Great Work, symbolizing decomposition and the stripping away of old forms.</note>, which a continual circulation of your water upon the earth will produce.

Now know that when you see your Water and your Body boil together, so as to thicken and solidify <note>original: "congeal"</note> one another, your science <note>science: Here meaning technical knowledge or mastery of the alchemical art.</note> is true. Then your Body, which thickens in this way, is not the same substance that you first put in, but a middle-state coagulate <note>coagulate: A substance that has changed from a fluid to a thickened, solid mass.</note>, an Adam's earth <note>original: "terra Adamica"; in alchemy, this refers to the "first matter" or the pure, primordial clay from which the first human was created.</note>, a slime <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for mud or slime, often used to describe the fertile, primary matter of the work.</note>...

## An Exposition upon

*Limus* <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for mud or slime, often referring to the "slime of the earth" or the primal matter from which life and substances originate.</note> and *Chaos* <note>original: "Chaos"; the initial, disordered state of matter before it is given a specific form.</note>, for when one form is taken away, a second necessarily follows immediately. Just as no body can have more than one form at any given time, it can also never be completely void of all form.

*And here a secret I will disclose to you,*
*Which is the foundation of all our secrets,*
*And if it is not known, you shall only lose*
*Your labor and costs, both great and small:*
*Take care, therefore, that you do not fall into error.*
*The more "Earth" <note>Earth: The solid, stable part of the alchemical matter.</note> you have, and the less moisture,*
*The sooner and better the dissolution <note>original: "Solution"; the process of a solid being dissolved into a liquid state.</note> you shall see.*

And here, take notice by the way that no dissolution is total before calcination <note>calcine: To heat a substance until it is reduced to a powder, usually the first step in purification.</note>; it is only partial. The "Water" <note>Water: The liquid or mercurial solvent used in the work.</note> dissolves as much of the Body as it can—enough that it separates its Spirit from its Body. However, because of the Body's perfection and strong compaction <note>compaction: The density or tightness of the substance's molecular structure.</note>, it encounters a great deal of difficulty before reaching a total resolution <note>resolution: The complete breaking down of a substance into its primary components.</note>. Therefore, the process putrefies <note>putrefies: To rot or decompose; a necessary stage in alchemy to break down the "gross" or impure parts of the matter.</note> whatever is most coarse <note>original: "gross"</note>, thus reducing it to atoms. Once it is refined <note>original: "subtilized"</note> beyond...

<summary>This page explains that matter must always possess a form and reveals a "secret" ratio—that using more solid "Earth" than liquid "moisture" facilitates a better dissolution. It distinguishes between partial dissolution and the total resolution of matter into atoms, which requires calcination and putrefaction.</summary>

...of true solution, which is (trust me) neither total nor proper until after putrefaction <note>Putrefaction: The stage of the alchemical process where the matter decomposes, typically turning black, which was considered essential for its "rebirth."</note>. I would never have told you this mystery <note>mystery: From the Latin 'mysterium,' referring to secret or specialized knowledge known only to initiates of the craft.</note> if the love of my neighbor had not compelled me. That opening of the Body <note>Body: The solid metallic or mineral substance being worked upon.</note> which occurs before is merely an opening of its pores, which lets our Water <note>Water: The alchemical solvent or "mercury" used to break down the solid material.</note> in; then, after "death" and "resurrection," the Mercury of Gold <note>original: "Mercury of Sol"; Sol (the Sun) is the alchemical name for Gold.</note> becomes visible to the eye, which before could only be distinguished by its effects.

> Behold how ice yields to water,
> And so it must, for it was water before;
> Just so, our water returns to earth,
> And water is thereby congealed forevermore:
> For according to all philosophers who were ever born,
> Each metal was once mineral water,
> Therefore, with water, they all turn back to water.

So then, our Body has moisture within itself, but this moisture is sealed away, just like water when it is frozen by the cold. But when the pores of the Body are opened by our Water, and its central fire is set at liberty, this internal fire of nature causes the Body to no longer be a solid body, but a true spirit <note>spirit: In alchemy, a "spirit" refers to a substance that has been refined into a volatile or gaseous state.</note>.

<summary>The author explains that true chemical dissolution only occurs after the material has undergone putrefaction, comparing the process to ice melting back into the water from which it originated.</summary>

In this same operation, the spirit is congealed <note>congealed: thickened or solidified; in alchemy, this refers to a gaseous or liquid substance becoming solid</note>, for the body has in it more virtue <note>original: "virtue"; here meaning power, potency, or inherent strength</note> than its two associates—that is, than the soul and the spirit. > *Things must therefore be rotated into other things,*
> *Until that Trinity is brought to perfect Unity.*

They have called their <term>Metals</term> *the Sun* <note>original: "Sol"; representing Gold</note> and *the Moon* <note>original: "Luna"; representing Silver</note> "Mountains," either for the sake of their location—since they are generally found in mountains—or by way of contrast; for just as mountains are the highest points above ground, these metals lie deepest under the ground. Similarly, they have also named them <term>Planets</term> because of certain similarities.

But the name matters less than the reality: take the <term>Body</term> which is *Gold*, and throw it into *Mercury*—a *Mercury* that is "bottomless," meaning its center can never be found except by the gold discovering its own. Govern them wisely with *Fire*, as your <term>Matter</term> requires. Then your *Gold* shall visibly liquefy in the *Fire*—that is, it will appear thin as if it were *Mercury* itself. It will swell, bubble, and boil until the moisture is resolved <note>original: "terminated"</note> by the Body into an impalpable <term>Powder</term> <note>a powder so fine it cannot be felt between the fingers</note>.

## Sir George Ripley’s Second Gate

Circulations—and every **Circulation** had these three periods—so now I tell you that these three periods are **Altitude**, **Latitude**, and **Profundity**. When **Altitude** and **Profundity** are united, they create **Latitude**, and thus our **Wheel** is turned around. The **Profundity** is the **Water** below, the **Altitude** is the **Vapor** or **Waters** above, and the union of these two results in a **Calx** <note>original: "Calx"; the fine powder or ash left after a metal has been burned or chemically decomposed</note>, which is **Latitude**. This is achieved through **Liquefaction**, **Sublimation**, and **Calcination**. **Liquefaction** dissolves and blends; **Sublimation** volatilizes, separates, and washes; and **Calcination** unites and fixes the substance.

*Knowing that your entrance shall be in the West,*
*Your passage onward to the North, if you do well,*
*And there your lights will lose their light entirely <note>original: "each deal"</note>,*
*For there you must remain for ninety nights,*
*In the darkness of purgatory <note>in alchemy, "purgatory" refers to a stage of intense purification where the material "dies" and turns black</note> without light.*

You must begin in the **West** and in the **Autumn**, which is **Barren**, for that is when the crops are gathered. Take then your **Gold**, which is the **Harvest** of nature’s works.

<page-num>214</page-num>

# An Exposition upon...

...this vessel then, which for the sake of comparison <note>original: "similitudes sake"</note> we call an **Egg**, must be so tightly closed when our **materials** are placed in it that the **spirits** <note>In alchemy, "spirits" refers to the volatile substances or vapors released during heating that must be contained to react.</note> cannot possibly escape, nor can the outside air get in; otherwise, our **work** would be spoiled.

Then you must know the proper measure of **firing**,
For if this is unknown, your **work** is entirely lost <note>original: "lost each deal"</note>.
Never let your **glass** vessel be hotter than you can stand to feel,
And always be able to hold it in your bare hand,
For fear of losing everything, as the **philosophers** <note>Referring to the ancient and medieval authorities on alchemy whose writings formed the basis of the craft.</note> have told us.

Once this is done, we then set our **vessel** and **matter** to the **fire** and let it stand untouched until the **work** is finished. Thus, the **philosopher** has nothing left to do then but watch his **glass** and the **operation** within it, and to manage his **fire** with skill <note>original: "artificially," meaning according to the rules of the "Art" of alchemy.</note>.

So then, once the **Stone** is set to work, the entire **mastery** consists of governing the external **fire**, which—as the **philosopher** says—either perfects or destroys everything. If your **fire** is too slow for lack of movement <note>original: "motion"; this refers to the circulation of heat or the intensity of the bellows.</note>, you will hardly ever see an end to the process; and if it is too big,

<page-num>215</page-num>

# Sir George Ripley's Second Gate

...big, you might happen to find yourself searching for your fortune in the ashes <note>A warning that if the fire is too hot, the glass vessel will explode, leaving the precious materials lost in the ashes of the furnace.</note>.

Therefore, do not be immoderate in governing the heat. For better security, do not let the neck of your **glass** be less than a span <note>A span is an old unit of measurement, roughly 9 inches (the width of an expanded hand).</note> in length, but as much longer as you see fit. For a **beginner** <note>original: "Tyro"</note>, the longer the neck is, the better he will work and with more security. However, the usual length we use is about 12 or 14 inches high. With this height established, arrange your furnace so that about 3 or 4 inches of the top of your glass protrudes through the cover of your **athanor** <term>Athanor: A self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a constant heat for long periods without constant attention.</term>. If you can touch or handle any part of that neck without being burned <note>original: "without hurt feel or suffer"</note>, do not fear your fire, but let the matter simmer <note>original: "stew him"</note> without worry. As long as your glass is strong, the faster the fire, the better.

Yet know that your furnace must be suitable for the task. Do not believe that the **philosophers** <note>Referring to the historical alchemical authorities.</note> formerly used our modern specialized furnaces; instead, they made them of brick or clay <note>original: "Earth"</note> with clay covers. These covers had holes to allow part of the necks of their glasses to stick out, over which they placed another cover that they could remove and set back on...

<page-num>216</page-num>

# An Exposition upon

...back on at their pleasure. This clay <note>original: "Earthen"</note> cover was not as reflective of heat as our modern iron covers are; however, the end of the **glass** that protruded through the hole in the cover could be handled without any injury. By being able to tolerate that heat in their hand, they judged the temperature of their fire. 

Therefore, in your **furnace**, let your cover or top be sealed <term>Luted: Sealed or coated with a substance like clay to make it airtight and heat-resistant.</term> with good clay <note>original: "Loam"</note> everywhere, at least half an inch thick. In this way, you will be sure not to have an excessively scalding heat in the hollow of your **nest** <term>Nest: The inner chamber or hollow part of the furnace where the vessel sits.</term>, which you would otherwise have. Thus, you may control your fire as you wish. 

You do not need to cover the necks of your glasses that protrude. By doing this, you will see this teaching of *Ripley* <note>George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), a famous English alchemist and author of *The Compound of Alchymy*.</note> proven true: your **work** <note>In alchemy, "the Work" refers to the entire process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> will proceed very successfully, and you will always be able to tolerate holding your glass in your hand. This is the true meaning behind the writings of all the **philosophers** <note>Historical alchemical authorities.</note>: to provide a definite rule so that you never exceed the proper temperature. That rule is: as long as you can bear to touch any part of your glass—provided your **nest** is covered and the ends of the glass necks protrude—you have not exceeded the safe limit of heat.

<sig>Tet</sig>
<vocab>Ripley, Glass, Furnace, Loam, Nest, Philosophers, heat, Cover, Work</vocab>

<summary>The author provides a practical rule for temperature control in alchemy: the heat is correct as long as the exposed neck of the glass vessel can still be touched by hand. It emphasizes using clay insulation to prevent the furnace from becoming too hot.</summary>

*Still attend further to my teaching:*
*Beware that you never open or move your glass,*
*From the beginning until you have reached the end;*
*If you do otherwise, your work may never succeed <note>original: "cheeve"</note>.*

And that this aligns with the thinking of all wise men is evident by their collective testimony and by the following words of Ripley. He says, "See that you do not open your glass, nor move it, from the beginning to the end of the work." Therefore, this method of testing the temperature of the glass by touch must be done without opening or moving it. For if the **seed** <note>The alchemical matter or "sulfur" that is expected to grow and transform.</note> is disturbed when it begins to grow <note>original: "vegetate"</note>, the work is undoubtedly ruined. At the very least, it will be so significantly weakened that it will hardly show you the true signs <note>Expected changes in color or state, like the "Black Crow's Head."</note> at the proper time.

Therefore, when you place your **egg** <note>The hermetically sealed glass vessel containing the alchemical ingredients.</note> in your **nest** <term>Nest: The heated chamber or sand-bath within the furnace.</term>, take care not to interfere with it until the **mastery** <note>The successful completion of the alchemical transformation.</note> is achieved—except perhaps with a wire or some similar tool, or with

...a hole in your cover, to secure the neck of your glass from shaking this way or that, which it would otherwise be very prone to do.

Thus I have briefly reviewed this Second Gate of Dissolution, which is essentially the same as Calcination and Separation; for through constant sublimation, the body is dissolved <note>original: "Solution of the Body"</note>, and eventually a solidifying <term>Congelation: The process where a liquid or gaseous "spirit" becomes a solid substance.</term> of the spirits occurs. By rising often, they reach a point where they will no longer ascend, but instead remain together at the bottom of the vessel, which is Conjunction. In this Conjunction, they swell, bubble, and boil until they calcine and rot <note>original: "putrefie"</note>. The "black earth," as fine and intangible as dust motes in a sunbeam <note>original: "Atoms of the Sun"</note>, is the highest stage of decomposition; this is a secret that has not been so clearly revealed by anyone before.

*The Sun has set; it is no wonder that the darkest night*
*Veils the crystal sky:*
*The Moon is eclipsed; it is no surprise that her light*
*Lies hidden from us.*
*The Sun has moved toward the Northern Pole,*
*And oh, what a change has been made!*

## Sir George Ripley’s Second Gate

The pearly drops are turned to a coal;
  All brightness fades away completely.
Is this bright Apollo <note>Apollo: In alchemy, a symbol for the Sun or Gold.</note>, whose glory displayed
  Such great luster?
Is this fair Phoebe <note>Phoebe: In alchemy, a symbol for the Moon or Silver.</note>, who before her light was hidden,
  Shone as bright as day?
Is this the King whose glory and renown
  Rang through all the world?
Is this the Queen who was known far and near?
  Oh, it is a wondrous thing
That such glory and such beauty should fade like this!
  That what previously shone
More brightly than the Tagus <note>Tagus: A river in Spain and Portugal famous in antiquity for its gold-bearing sands; used here to represent wealth and brilliance.</note> should so soon be made
  More foul than it was ever beautiful! <note>original: "fine"</note>
The earth melts, the heavens drop down rain;
  The rocks, which give way <note>original: "relent"</note>,
They seem like water, then condense again
  Until all their moisture is spent.
To ashes they return, for they were dust;
  This dust springs from water.
Therefore, at length, they melt into clear water,
  Which turns everything into spirits.
The nest is earth; therefore, they will congeal <term>Congeal: To solidify or thicken from a fluid state into a solid "body."</term>
  Into sparkling, pearly dew,
Shining like a delicate pearl, upon which
  A dry and new body gradually forms <note>original: "doth steal"</note>.

And then the particles, like motes in the sunbeams <note>original: "Atoms of the Sun"; refers to tiny particles of matter becoming visible as they reach a state of extreme fineness.</note>,
  Appear in their finest form.
Rejoice, for now you have completed half your journey,
  And you have no cause for fear.

Continue until you see the sparkling red <note>The red stage, or *Rubedo*, is the final major phase in the alchemical "Great Work," symbolizing perfection and the completion of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>,
  Oh, what a happy sight to see!
By this, you are led to the Royal Palace <note>The "Royal Palace" is a common alchemical metaphor for the successful completion of the work or the attainment of the Stone.</note>,
  Where you shall be happy forever.

Happy are those who do not fail to find
  The new, rising Sun;
Even happier are those who, with a renewed mind,
  Find rest in God alone.

### The Third Gate Opened,
## Which is
# SEPARATION.

*Separation divides each part from the others,*
*The subtle from the gross <note>original: "gross"; meaning the thick, heavy, or impure matter</note>, the thick from the thin,*

*But set aside manual separation,*
*For that belongs to fools and yields little fruit <note>meaning it produces little result or success</note>.*

*But in our separation, Nature does not cease <note>original: "blin"; an archaic term for stop or desist</note>,*
*Making a division of elemental qualities,*
*Until they are all turned into a fifth degree <note>referring to the "quintessence," the purest and most highly refined state of matter</note>.*

Having now passed through two of the twelve gates, I have come to the third, which is <term>Separation</term>. This begins as soon as the materials have been circulated for long enough that they begin to take hold of one another. The ancient sages <note>referring to the historical and legendary authorities of alchemy</note> have named this operation the <term>Division of Elements</term>, which they say must afterwards be joined in a perpetual union. This

<page-num>224</page-num>
<header>An Exposition upon</header>

Separation is called by others the "Extraction of Natures," and the parts separated are compared to two dragons: the one winged, and the other without wings <note>a common alchemical allegory where the winged dragon represents the volatile or spiritual part, and the wingless dragon represents the fixed or physical part</note>.

*Artephius* <note>a legendary alchemical author, often claimed to have lived for over a thousand years</note>, who for his age and integrity was the most eminent next to *Hermes* <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of alchemy</note>, calls this separation the "Key of the Work." According to him, this is a <term>sublimation</term> <note>the process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note> in a continuous vapor. This allows what is heavenly and subtle to ascend upward—that is, to the upper part of the vessel—and there take on the nature of a "heavenly body" or spirit. Meanwhile, what is gross <note>thick, heavy, or impure</note> remains below in the nature of an "earthly body." The goal of our mastery is to bring bodies that are compact and dry to become a spiritual smoke <note>original: "fume"</note>, which can only be achieved by sublimation, and division or separation.

Therefore, our separation is not to be understood as many foolish alchemists interpret it. They boast much of their "elements," which are actually manual—done by physical handiwork—where the glass vessel is removed, altered, or replaced every

It emphasizes that true alchemical separation is a natural sublimation into "spiritual smoke" rather than a crude, manual process.</summary>

<sig>P 2</sig>

every time. Nor are our Separations made by filter, or by means of a separator <note>original: *per tritorium*; a Latin term for a vessel or funnel used to separate liquids of different densities</note>, as many imagine who do not know the nature of our Work and therefore fall into such foolish fancies.

Nature, then, performs everything in our Work. Like a skilled craftsman <note>original: "curious Artificer"</note>, she creates no confused mixtures. Instead, she first causes the moisture to ascend. Because this moisture cannot escape the vessel, it condenses into drops and descends until it eventually begins to be energized <term>acuated</term> <note>original: "acuated"; in alchemy, this means to make a substance more chemically active, sharp, or potent through repeated contact</note> by the "Body" below. Naturally, all homogeneous moisture that is repeatedly distilled <term>cohobated</term> <note>original: "cohobated"; the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid and pouring it back over the solid residue to concentrate its virtues</note> upon a physical substance with which it has an affinity becomes energized by it. 

Gold, then, is a "Body" in which the active qualities of heat and dryness are greater than in the *Mercury*. When the *Mercury* is repeatedly distilled over it, it begins to become slightly more fiery or hot. Then the exhalations <note>vapors or fumes</note> become more airy <note>original: "Aërial"</note>, whereas before they were more watery. Through continued distillation, the "Water" partakes more and more of the solar nature <note>original: "Solary nature"; referring to the properties of gold, which is associated with the Sun/Sol</note>, until finally this heat or *Sulphur* <note>in alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle</note> impregnates the *Mercury*, causing it to congeal into a new Body.

<summary>This page explains that true alchemical separation is a natural, internal process of evaporation and condensation within a sealed vessel, rather than a manual filtration. Through repeated distillation, the volatile Mercury is "energized" by the properties of Gold until it congeals into a new substance.</summary>

So then, this is the method of our Operation: Earth (which is Gold <note>original: *Sol*</note>) is boiled in our *Mercury* using such a heat that the *Mercury* may constantly ascend as smoke <note>vapor</note> and descend in drops, while the "Body" below remains liquid and boiling. Then the Water shall dry up, beneath which blackness is hidden; when the Water is dried up, this blackness shall appear like a Crow's Bill <note>original: "Crows Bill"; a traditional alchemical term for the deep black color produced during the *Nigredo* stage of the work, signaling the first successful stage of transformation</note>.

Then this powder shall soften <note>original: "relent"</note> again, and after forty days of decomposition <note>original: "rotting"</note> without fumes, it shall send up a smoke again. This smoke shall rise and fall <note>original: "ascend and descend"</note> for so long until the whole substance is made volatile and airy <note>original: "Aerial"</note>; then the black color shall vanish, and the white shall appear.

It shall then be Fire, whose "nurse is the Earth" <note>This is a famous phrase from the *Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus*, a foundational alchemical text</note>. At that point, you have the four elements—that is, you have redistilled <term>cohobated</term> <note>a process of repeatedly pouring a liquid back onto the solid residue from which it was distilled to concentrate its power</note> your essential substances <note>original: "Natures"</note> to the highest degree of perfection of the White Stone <note>the intermediate stage of the Philosopher’s Stone, capable of transmuting base metals into silver</note>. 

Then you can go no further in this direction, but must go back and turn the same wheel <note>repeat the cycle of the process</note> until you have attained the Red Stone <note>the final, most potent form of the Philosopher’s Stone, capable of transmuting metals into gold</note>. Thus you have the true principles and operation of our great *elixirs*, both red and white. If you once possess these, you have riches enough and need no more in this life.

This, even if no more were said on the subject, might be enough to show you the truth of our genuine separation. Yet because the philosophers <note>the master alchemists of history</note> have spoken much of it—and indeed, the entire work is to cause the rising <note>original: "ascension"</note>...

<note>This verse is from George Ripley’s *The Compound of Alchymy*. "Blowing at the coals" was a derogatory reference to "puffers"—unskilled alchemists who used excessive heat and bellows, unlike the true philosopher who used a gentle, steady heat.</note>

...circulation, a real change of the ascending moisture <note>original: "Humidity"</note>. The first substance to appear will be white and will remain so for a long time; this is called Water or Phlegm <term>Phlegm: in alchemy, a watery, tasteless distillate representing the element of water</term>. Afterward, the Water will become colored and rise up the sides of the vessel. This is called *Oil* <note>original: "Oyl"</note>. This *Oil* is incombustible, for it is the true *Sulphur* of Gold, and it is therefore as permanent as the *Mercury*.

Yet do not be mistaken, nor imagine that because we speak of incombustible *Oil*, our Work is to be performed with a wind-furnace <note>original: "Fire of a Wind"</note>, an oven, or bellows (as some foolishly imagine). Such people think they must burn up everything combustible until only the incombustible *Oil* is left. In reality, all of our volatile substance is turned into an incombustible solid state <term>fixity: the quality of being non-volatile and resistant to change by fire</term> through a moderate boiling <term>decoction</term> in our secret *Athanor* <term>Athanor: a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a uniform, constant heat over a long period</term>. Even at its highest strength <note>original: "vigoration"</note>, the heat of this furnace is only a very dim red—hardly visible—and at its lowest degree, it is not even half that strong.

This is a heat which is friendly to the Bodies <note>original: "Bodies" – referring to the chemical substances or metals being worked upon</note>, for it causes the Spirit <term>Spirit: the volatile substance or essence of the matter</term> to ascend, and yet allows it to return; and by reason of its ascending and returning, the Matter below stands continually moist, and boils with a perpetual motion and exhalation, which ascends and returns day and night, every hour and minute, without intermission.

Leavened dough; and from that time onward the compound shall grow drier and drier, eventually becoming pitch-black atoms, or an impalpable powder <term>impalpable: so fine that no grit or texture can be felt</term>, and the fumes shall cease for six weeks.

Therefore, be patient during the decoction <term>decoction: the process of heating a substance in a liquid to extract its essence or transform it</term>, and wait with a great deal of confidence until you see your "Water." This water at first rises up white and phlegmatic <note>original: "flegmatick" – in alchemy, this refers to a watery, tasteless distillate representing the element of water</note>, but then it begins to change color, and the exhalations <term>exhalations: vapors or fumes produced by heat</term> within the glass begin to appear discolored. 

Then continue your decoction until the "cloud" which has been conceived is brought forth. In this operation, be sure that the seeds <note>original: "Seeds" – the essential masculine and feminine principles within the matter</note> begin to mingle; they will give you a sign of the beginning of the Conjunction of Natures <term>Conjunction of Natures: the alchemical union of opposites, such as "sulfur" and "mercury"</term>. That sign is the gilding of the inside of the glass vessel, as if it were covered with leaves of pure gold.

Continue your decoction still further until the "Earth" <note>original: "Earth" – the solid residue or "body" remaining at the bottom of the vessel</note> begins to appear at the bottom, and the moisture of the compound begins to end in dryness. The resulting color will be black, which is a sure sign of your correct progress, and without which you can never achieve the Mastery <term>Mastery: the successful completion of the Great Work, or the Philosopher's Stone</term>.

Remember that in this calcination <term>calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or ash</term>, you have a portion of "water" in the upper part of your vessel <term>vessel: the glass container or apparatus used in the experiment</term> that did not descend. *Separation thus must you oftentimes make,*
*Dividing your "waters" into two parts,*
*So that you take the subtle from the gross,*

But to return to our work of sublimation <term>sublimation: the process where a solid turns into vapor and then back into a solid, used here to purify the substance</term>, which is—as was mentioned before—the key to the entire work. In this way, the "waters" are divided from the "waters"—that is, the waters above from those which are below <note>This is a reference to the Creation story in Genesis 1:7, used here as a metaphor for the separation of vapors from the liquid at the bottom of the flask</note>. # . *Sir George Ripley's Third Gate.*
<note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was a famous English alchemist. His "gates" refer to the twelve stages of the alchemical process described in his work, *The Compound of Alchemy*.</note>

This <note>Continuing from the previous page</note> congeals and runs down the sides of the glass in drops like veins, while part remains still below with the "body" <term>body: the solid or physical matter in the alchemical vessel</term>, and boils visibly and unceasingly with it.

Through this work, you have the subtle or thin parts of the "body" and the thin parts of the "water" <term>water: the liquid solvent or mercurial substance used in the experiment</term> ascend and mingle. At the same time, the gross <term>gross: the thick, impure, or heavy matter</term> part of the body and the gross part of the water are mixed below—the one by subliming together, and the other by boiling together. In this way, your body below is composed of two parts: the most fixed <term>fixed: a substance that has become stable and will no longer evaporate when exposed to fire</term> parts of *Sol* <note>original: "Sol" — Latin for "Sun," representing Gold or the masculine principle in alchemy</note>, combined with the grosser parts of *Lunaria* <note>original: "Lunaria" — Latin relating to the "Moon," representing Silver or the feminine principle</note>. Your water is also made of two parts: the soul of *Sol* and the spirit of *Lunaria*, which is the true mystical basis of fixation <term>fixation: the process of making a volatile substance stable so it can withstand heat without evaporating</term>.

Thus, by subliming into a continual vapor whatever is spiritual and heavenly—both in the water and in the body—the lighter elements ascend and take on the nature of a "spirit" in the upper part of the glass. Meanwhile, what is more gross, earthy, and corporeal <term>corporeal: having a physical body; material</term> will settle at the bottom and take on the nature of a "body." Once the soul has been separated from it, the color of this body will be as black as pitch <note>The "black as pitch" stage is known as *nigredo*, representing the symbolic death or "putrefaction" of the matter before its eventual purification and rebirth.</note>.

This body is an intermediate substance between the solid "body" and the liquid "water"—it is an alkaline substance <note>original: "Lixivius" — referring to salts or lye extracted from ashes</note>, a new kind of body, or "Adamic earth" <note>original: "Adamica terra" — a reference to the red earth from which Adam was created, symbolizing the pure, primal matter of the Great Work</note>. It is a middle ground <note>original: "medium"</note> between what is fixed <term>fixed: a substance that has become stable and will no longer evaporate when exposed to heat</term> and what is not fixed. It is not so stable as to be equal to gold <note>original: "Sol"</note>, nor yet as volatile as mercury, but it is stable enough to endure the heat required for this work and to suffer all the "pain and woe" of our "purgatory" <note>purgatory: here used as a metaphor for the intense purification and "suffering" of the matter as it is heated in the vessel</note>, where it remains for six weeks without producing fumes or vapor.

But as for the "spirit" <term>spirit: the volatile, vaporous, or "soul-like" part of the substance</term>, that is a delicate thing. It is unable to endure the fire; instead, it flies from the heat and remains in the uppermost part of the glass vessel. As long as the fumes rise, the ascending vapors continue to meet those already at the top until they eventually form large drops and fall back down. When the fumes stop, as much of the spirit as the curve <note>original: "Concave"</note> of the glass can hold without it running down stays at the top until the process of calcination <term>calcination: the reduction of a solid to a fine powder through prolonged heating</term> is completely finished. Then, the spirits are drawn back down by a magnetic attraction <note>original: "Magnetical virtue"</note>. Therefore, great care must be taken lest the Spirits escape <note>original: "exhale"</note>. They will do so unless the glass has a strong seal <note>original: "guard"</note>. First, they are subtle; second, they ascend with great force <note>original: "impetus"</note> because of our fire. This fire must cause the lower parts to boil and move continuously <note>original: "inferiora ebullire & moveri continuò"</note>, and the lower parts to circulate at every moment <note>original: "inferiora circulari, quolibet momento"</note>. Thirdly, during the stage of rotting <term>putrefaction: the decomposition of the substance within the vessel, a necessary stage in the Great Work</term>, the Body and Spirits have a very subtle scent, which must also be kept inside.

To prevent all of this, you shall make your stopper <note>original: "stopple"</note> as solid as any part of your glass. Let the glass be strong, as previously mentioned, and the neck long and thick. You should melt the neck shut using a lamp or coals, closing it well without twisting <note>original: "wringing"</note> it too much—twisting makes the glass brittle. Once it is pinched <note>original: "nipt"</note> shut, keep it in the same heat and turn it back and forth in the clear flame; the glass will form a surface <note>original: "superficies"</note> just as perfectly sealed and smooth as any other part of the vessel.

...the Body of Man, I never tried myself, nor gave it to another, nor do I believe did any of them <note>original: "they"</note>. For it is the true *Tree of Life* <note>original: "Arbor vitæ"</note>, which answers the universal desires of those who possess it in this form; for besides its curative virtue <term>virtue: an inherent power or medicinal property</term>, which it possesses in a wonderful, miraculous way, it can penetrate even to our constitutive principles <term>constitutive principles: the fundamental elements or "seeds" that make up the human body</term>, which no other mineral medicine can do. 

Though Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous Swiss physician and alchemist</note> boasts much of his *Renovating and Restoring medicines* <note>original: "Renovantia & Restaurantia"</note>—which we are familiar with, being masters of his secret *Alkahest* <term>Alkahest: a hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists, capable of dissolving any substance into its primary matter</term>, about which I will write a specific treatise if I live—yet it is not his *Hematina* <term>Hematina: medicinal preparations derived from blood</term>, nor his *Arcana* <term>Arcana: secret or hidden remedies believed to have great power</term>, nor his *Elixirs*, nor his *Essences*, nor any of his secrets (which are surely noble medicines) that can reach the root of life as this one can and will. 

For it performs all; it only cannot prevail against the appointment of God <note>i.e., the inevitable nature of death decreed by God</note>. Otherwise, were it not for that divine decree, it could really keep a man immortal; for it renews youth and retards age.

## SIR G. RIPLEY's Third Gate

These, then, are the circumstantial qualities of our Water: it is pure, clean, and very bright; it is quick <note>meaning "living" or "mobile"</note> and very fluent, without moistening <term>humectation: the act of wetting or making something damp; here it refers to the "mercurial" quality of a liquid that does not stick to or wet the hands</term>. It is the only profitable subject that we can choose for this Art, and whatever else in the world might be taken in hand besides this is nothing but a deception <note>original: "fallacious"</note>.

*It is a marvelous thing in its nature,* <note>original: "in kind"</note>
*And without it, nothing can be done;*
*Therefore Hermes called it his "Wind,"* <note>A reference to the *Emerald Tablet* of Hermes Trismegistus: "The wind carried it in its belly."</note>
*For it flies upward from Sun and Moon,* <note>In alchemy, the Sun and Moon represent Gold and Silver.</note>
*And makes our Stone fly with it soon,*
*Reviving the dead, and giving life*
*To Sun and Moon, Husband and Wife.*

It is of a wonderful composition—yea, so wonderful that if you were to know it only by hearsay <note>original: "relation"</note>, you could not believe it. Study, therefore, only to know it, for it is the very hinge on which all perfection turns; it is that which the wise men never revealed except in figures and metaphors.

<page-num>253</page-num>

### Sir George Ripley's Third Gate

The sublimation appears oily <note>original: "pinguous"</note>. The middle agent <note>original: "*medium*"</note> of this extraction is Water, because our Water and the <term>Sulphur</term> <note>the active, combustible principle in alchemy</note> are of the same nature <note>original: "homogeneal"</note>.

Therefore, in this circulation, there are two things to be considered: the bottom and the top. The bottom is not solely the Body of **Gold** <note>original: "*Sol*"</note>; if it were, it would not remain liquid, flow, boil, and bubble as it does. It is certain, then, that the Body retains part of the Water which is thicker—a thickness caused by digestion and mixture. This grosser part of the Water is joined with the Body but not yet perfectly united.

The uppermost part is not composed only of the Water, nor is it the entirety of the Water. Rather, it is a certain subtle portion of your first **Vinegar** <note>likely "philosophical vinegar," a specialized solvent or Mercury used by alchemists</note> which contains the most pure part of your Gold. This Gold is elevated <note>original: "sublimed"</note> along with the Vinegar, and together they create a middle agent of great fieriness. 

Because of this mixture, both the uppermost part and the settling <note>original: "subsident"</note> part are brought to a middle state <note>original: "mean"</note>, where they cling to one another. Therefore, at this time and in this operation, our Body is called the Body of both...

Now the Water which you mix with it has this power <note>original: "virtue"</note>: to open its pores, and then the Water of the Body will as naturally agree and ascend with your Water of Life <note>a potent alchemical solvent or purified spirit</note>, which you added to it, just as one water will join with another.

Now, as the Sun <note>original: "*Sol*"; in alchemy, the symbol for gold or the masculine principle</note> has a hidden Spirit, so has our **Mercury**, which is invisible within it; for to the sight it appears like other **Mercury**, only a little brighter, but in effect they

they differ wonderfully. This Spirit will as naturally unite with the Soul, or fiery part of the Body, as Light will mix with Light. Then the dense <note>original: "gross"</note> part of the Body, and of the Water at the bottom of the Vessel, will be brought to putrefy <note>decompose and turn black, a necessary stage in the Great Work</note> in the absence of the Soul and Spirit.

So then these two fiery natures, being of the same kind <note>original: "homogeneous"</note>, will readily mix and rise together <note>original: "sublime"</note> in the form of a white smoke or vapor, as the noble *Artephius* <note>a legendary alchemical author, often cited for his works on the longevity of life and the philosopher's stone</note> says. There, condensing in the top of the vessel—that is, around the sides and in the hollow <note>original: "concave"</note> of the glass—it will return again and circulate up and down until it has destroyed the solidity of the Body. This process makes it "no body," but instead draws up <note>original: "subliming"</note> what is subtle and turns what is earthy and resistant into ashes, or an extremely fine <note>original: "impalpable"</note> powder, by calcination <note>the process of reducing a substance to a powdery or friable state through heat</note>.

And after putrefaction is complete by circulation, the most stable <note>original: "fixed"</note> part—which is called the <term>Body of Fixion</term> <note>the essential, permanent part of the matter that remains after volatile elements have been transformed</note>, the essential and most permanent part of both Body and Water—will be weightily lifted up and carried aloft into the air.

And without this separation and division,

# An Exposition upon
<note>original: "An Expofition upon" – likely referring to Sir George Ripley's "The Compound of Alchemy"</note>

...well be accounted a Master of our Secrets. We have, indeed, four Fires, which is one more than **Artephius** <note>Artephius: A legendary alchemist, possibly from the 12th century, to whom several influential Hermetic texts were attributed</note> numbered, though he intended to include it. 

The most noble Fire is **Natural**, which is that which we seek to have multiplied; that is the **Sulphur** <note>Sulphur: In alchemy, the active, masculine principle representing heat and dryness</note> of Gold, or rather its fiery **Tincture** <note>Tincture: The spiritualized essence or "soul" of a substance that has the power to transform other materials</note>. It is this which we seek for, and we use **Mercury** <note>Mercury: Often called "Quicksilver"; the passive, feminine principle representing fluidity and moisture</note> for the sake of **Sol** <note>original: "Sol" – Latin for "Sun," the alchemical name for Gold</note>.

Our next Fire is our **Fire Against Nature**, and that is the Fire of our Water <note>Water: Here referring to the corrosive solvent or "mercurial water" used to break down the gold</note> which is to be corrupted; it is through this corruption that **Multiplication** <note>Multiplication: The stage in the Great Work where the power and quantity of the alchemical medicine is increased</note> is achieved.

The third Fire is **Unnatural**, which is the mixture of these two Fires while they are in their action and passion <note>action and passion: Philosophical terms for the active and passive states of substances as they react with one another</note>, and neither one actually predominates over the other.

Now, to give you a reason for the names of these Fires, know that **Mineral Fire** is **Sulphur**, which is hot and dry. It is the death of the **Mineral Tree** <note>Mineral Tree: A metaphor for the natural growth and maturation of metals within the earth</note>; that is, it is the cause of **coagulating** <note>coagulating: The process of turning a liquid substance into a solid</note>, or taking away the "flux" (fluidity) of the **Mercury**, which is cold and moist. This is apparent in Gold, for it is a coagulated, perfect Body, fixed and permanent in all trials.

<summary>This page defines the first three of the "Four Fires" in alchemy: the Natural Fire (the essence of gold), the Fire Against Nature (the corrosive solvent), and the Unnatural Fire (their combined reaction). It explains that the "Mineral Fire" of sulphur acts by solidifying the fluid mercury into the permanent body of gold.</summary>

For although Gold is a compact and dry earthy body, no one should think that it became what it is without the power of a **Seed**. This seed is not destroyed when the metal reaches perfection; it is merely sealed up. This Seed is a fiery form of Light, which nothing in the world lacks. Therefore, it would be a great anomaly <note>original: *Anomalum*</note> if it were missing only in metals, which are the most excellent of all earthly <note>original: *sublunary*</note> bodies.

Between these two fires—during the time they act and react upon one another—an intermediate state <note>original: *medium*</note> is created that shares the qualities of both. Because this state is not entirely natural, yet not completely against nature, it is called **unnatural**.

The duration of this "unnatural fire" lasts from the moment the Body begins to open and its colors start to change. In short, it lasts through the entire rule of **Saturn** <note>Saturn: the alchemical stage of *nigredo*, or blackness, symbolizing the death and decay of the old form</note> and part of the rule of **Jupiter** <note>Jupiter: the stage where the material begins to change from black to gray or multi-colored, signifying the start of purification</note>. This covers the whole process of **Putrefaction** <note>putrefaction: the chemical decomposition or "rotting" of the substance necessary for rebirth</note> and as much of the **Ablution** <note>ablution: the "washing" or purifying of the matter through heat</note> as is needed until the **Dove** <note>the Dove: a symbol for the *albedo* or white stage, indicating the matter is becoming pure, spiritualized, and stable</note> begins to prevail.

### How the Gate of Separation Must Be Won

Thus, I have finished explaining this Gate of Separation, which might be sufficient on its own, for it contains everything. However, because the wise men <note>original: "Wise men"; referring to the historical alchemists or "philosophers" who sought the Stone</note> have performed many operations <term>chemical or symbolic processes</term> to hide their secrets, and have scattered their ideas here and there throughout every "Gate" or operation—sometimes placing them at the beginning and sometimes at the end in order to puzzle the unwary—I must, to make this treatise <term>a formal written work on a subject</term> complete, move through the remaining steps with as much brevity and plainness as I can.

### SIR GEORGE RIPLEY's Third Gate.

*I shall now sing a pleasant elegy <term>a poem of serious reflection, typically a lament for the dead</term>,*

*A wife*
*Lost her life*

*To see her dead who gave him life.*

*Beheld a sight the like of which was never seen.*
*She cries*

*For so long that her tears*

*The queen sank, but the king swam.*

*Perplexed him so much,*
*That starting up, not knowing what was wrong with him <note>original: "what him ail'd"</note>,*
*He was sorely troubled <note>original: "did vex"</note>;*

# The Fourth Gate Opened,
## Which is
## CONJUNCTION.

After the chapter on natural separation,
By which the elements of our Stone <term>Stone: The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance sought by alchemists to transform base metals and achieve spiritual perfection.</term> are separated <note>original: "dissevered"</note>,
The chapter follows here regarding secret conjunction,
Which joins opposing <note>original: "repugnant"</note> natures into perfect unity,
And binds <note>original: "knitteth"</note> them together so that none may flee from the others;
When they shall be tested <note>original: "examinate"</note> by the fire,
They will be so securely joined <note>original: "conjugante"</note> together.

Having gone through the chapter on separation in a plain style, we shall now come to the life of the whole work, which is conjunction. For we do not seek something that can be separated, but something that can endure all trials, where the parts are impossible to be... <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword "fepa-" suggests the next word is "separated."</note>

<summary>This page introduces the "Fourth Gate" of alchemy, known as Conjunction, where the previously separated elements of the "Stone" are unified into a permanent substance that can withstand the test of fire.</summary>

For separation is only the middle movement by which we pass from the singular <note>original: "unary"</note> simplicity of Gold to the thousand-fold *more than* <note>original: "plusquam"</note> perfection of our Stone. Before this can be attained, there must be a loosening of the framework <term>Compages: The structural binding or "glue" that holds a physical body together.</term> of the Body, so that the Spiritual Fire or Tincture may be set free. This, then, is the benefit of our Water: it does not only reduce, open, and soften <note>original: "mollifie"</note> our Body and cause it to release its seed, but it is actually recongealed with the fermenting power of this seminal influence of Gold, so that it becomes, together with the Body, one new and perpetually united Body.

### Sir G. Ripley's fourth Gate.

...cannot imagine any other *Mercury* than that *Mercury* which is bought from druggists, which they take and sublime <note>original: "sublime"; the process of heating a substance until it vaporizes and then becomes solid again, used here as a cleaning method.</note> in various ways to make it clean.
